# Project incident log (`agent-factory`)

> Institutional memory: the incidents behind the rules in CLAUDE.md. **Log a one-line entry
> here the moment a CLAUDE.md rule bites for real** — which section/rule it confirms, the
> concrete trigger, the fix — don't defer it to a handoff doc; a lesson recorded only there
> WILL be re-learned the expensive way (deep-work retrospective rule). GENERAL rules distilled
> from an incident still get baked into the matching skill/CLAUDE.md section; this file carries
> the full incident detail. Ported structure from `trading-system/docs/INCIDENT-LOG.md`.

**2026-09-24 23:4xZ — the coordinator's probe OOM-killed the vLLM `qwen` engine; systemd restarted the service (AF-AP-201).** While measuring the Qwen Jev adapter's transport (task #241), a request with `prompt_logprobs: 0` went through OmniRoute to vLLM: a full-vocabulary log-softmax per prompt token needed 758 MiB with 148 MiB free, the EngineCore died at 23:45:08Z, and systemd restarted `qwen.service` at 23:45:21Z; it answered again at 23:50:00Z, about 5 minutes down. No lane was live and the Laya evaluation runs on the CPU; any owner session on the local model lost its in-flight request. Rule confirmed: never touch the owner's servers without their say-so; a request option is a touch when it can exhaust the server's memory. Fix: AF-AP-201 (registry row, AP_SCREEN tell, tests), PC-BRIDGE.md's vLLM section, and the probe that reads the prompt ids another way (none sent since).

**2026-09-24 23:2xZ — an owner-run `sudo dnf install python3.11-devel` hung at dnf's `Is this ok [y/N]` prompt: the owner's terminal does not take the typed answer ("I have had this issue many times").** The pre-answered form, `sudo dnf install -y python3.11-devel`, completed in a second terminal; the first process (pid 1699180, pts/4) stayed at its prompt, harmless. Rule (CLAUDE.md's sudo sentence, PC-BRIDGE.md's preflight bullet): every owner-run package command is written with `-y`. Not a code defect: no registry row.

**2026-09-24 23:1xZ — the first live GPU window: the window worked, every job failed on a missing `python3.11-devel`; the CPU smokes never took the GPU path.** `scripts/gpu_window.sh` (GW1-R2, MERGE-READY-WITH-FOLLOWUPS) ran window 20260924T230902Z: stop 23:09:04Z, the GPU free at once (255 MiB), job 1 (G0, the cached venv) rc 1 in 5 s, job 2 (the no-cache fallback) rc 1 in 6 s, job 3 (the head-mode Laya run) rc 1 in 21 s, start 23:09:37Z, back 23:10:57Z (80 s), close rc 1: qwen was down 1 min 53 s. The cause, the same in all three: Triton compiles a small C shim for its CUDA driver on first GPU use, and `gcc` needs `Python.h`; the PC has `python3.11` 3.11.14 and `python3-devel` for 3.13 but not `python3.11-devel` (`/usr/include/python3.11/` holds only `pyconfig-64.h`), so fla fell back ("Triton is not supported on current platform") and the forward failed, and the Laya trainer failed the same way. A diagnostic after the window (no install, a throwaway TRITON_CACHE_DIR, no GPU memory): with the uv-installed CPython 3.11.13 headers on C_INCLUDE_PATH, the shim compiles in both venvs (triton 3.3.1 and 3.8.0), so the headers are the only gap. Rule confirmed, and bitten in a new form (the 19:2xZ entry's first item): the smoke before a scarce run must take the scarce run's OWN path. The CPU plumbing run and the trainer's CPU smoke never reach a GPU-only first-use step, and that step needs no GPU memory, so it could have been probed while qwen was up. Baked into build-loop step 4. The fix needs the owner (sudo) or the owner's say-so for the header path.


**2026-09-24 21:4xZ — the new stale-id push check caught a recurring defect on its first run, and fixed three of its own blind spots first.** (1) Task #243's check refused the 21:3xZ push: `sandbox-kit/VENDORED-MANIFEST.md:4` ("Generated from commit") cited the local HEAD of the manifest regen, which the push rewrote (18e391b to 4ed48ac). Every regen before a push has done this: the manifests of 4bfa4da and 21288bd on origin cite 67d1a256f8fd and d90fe6558439, which do not exist there (the drift comparison ignores the line, so nothing noticed). The line now cites 4ed48ac; task #246 makes the generator record a stable id. (2) Before that push, a re-read of the committed checker found AF-AP-200: `diff.noprefix`, a quoted path and an added line starting `++` each hid a citation (three tests red on it, green after the fix). Rule confirmed: re-read your own diff adversarially before a push (deep-work Phase 5: done means a hostile reviewer failed to break it); the independent verify (VERIFY-T243-245) runs now.

**2026-09-24 21:1xZ — AF-AP-145 met twice in the GPU window script; a nested `timeout` escapes a group kill.** (1) `scripts/gpu_window.sh` was written with an EXIT-trap restore and INT/TERM handlers that did not open with `trap '' INT TERM`: VERIFY-GW1 F-1 (a signal during the restore exited 130 with the service down). The GW1-R1 repair put the ignore in `restore` only. The coordinator's sweep then found the residual that AF-AP-145 already names: a second TERM 4.75 ms after the first skipped the start (1 of 49), and a burst of TERMs across the abort skipped it in 3 of 3 runs; with the ignore also first in each handler, 3 of 3 green (24 windows). The screen line existed (`python3 scripts/ap_screen.py scripts/gpu_window.sh`: 3 hits on the first script, 2 on the repair, 0 on the fixed copy), but no hook runs it on a shell edit: `edit-snapshot.py` returns early for a non-`.py` file (task #245). Rule confirmed: a registered signature that no hook runs is not in the loop (CLAUDE.md, code intelligence: "an instrument that is not in a script on the path is not in the loop"). The second repair waits for VERIFY-GW1-R1, which runs on the committed blob. (2) While writing the window's jobs file: a nested `timeout` without `--foreground` makes its own process group, and the window's TERM and KILL miss it (bash 5.2.21: `timeout -k 1 2 bash -c "cd /tmp && timeout 30 sleep 31; true"` left `sleep 31` alive after rc 124; with `--foreground` it was gone; when bash could exec the last command it was gone too). VERIFY-GW1 F-4's class; the jobs file uses `--foreground`; recorded on #74.

**2026-09-24 20:0xZ — a bucket stamp ahead of the clock, inside the slack.** A wiki live-state block stamped `20:1xZ` was committed at 20:08:53Z: its earliest instant (20:10:00) was 67 s ahead, and `scripts/stamp_check.py` passed it because every stamp got the 120 s slack. The stamp was typed before `date -u` ran in the same command. Corrected by a follow-up commit; `stamp_check.py` now gives a bucket stamp (`HH:MxZ`) no slack (it already spans ten minutes), while the minute and ISO forms keep it (`tests/test_stamp_check.py::test_a_bucket_stamp_gets_no_slack`; the old checker fails it). AF-AP-37's future-stamp class; the rule "run `date -u` before composing a stamp" stands.

**2026-09-24 19:2xZ — three small bites while preparing the GPU window.** (1) AF-AP-121's class (availability is not effect): the 18:0xZ ledger said `~/venv-rwkv` "imports cleanly", and the plan treated that as a ready stack; the first real forward, run on the PC CPU, failed in fla 0.3.0's cache under transformers 4.57.6. **2026-09-24 18:3xZ — a hollow red the owner caught, and two verify rounds.** (1) The owner asked whether RWKV-7's yes/no limit was real. It was not: simple-jev's noul prefill ends with a space, and the RWKV World tokenizer merges that space with the digit, so the compiler's boundary check refused; without the space all nine labels are single stable tokens (measured on the PC CPU; AF-AP-195, baked into anti-hollow-green tactic 7 as the hollow-red corollary). Rule confirmed: CLAUDE.md #1, the hollow claim in prose (a false limit is one). (2) VERIFY-JT3-R1 N-1: the search hook answered a comma-separated Grep glob as one glob, "rg: 0 hits" (AF-AP-194); JT3-R2, the one final repair D-031 allows, passes such a glob through; the same verifier reverifies. (3) VERIFY-FT1 found AF-AP-193's class in FT1's error path (F-3, cut before redact), and F-1/F-6 (a non-finite checkpoint saved with rc 0; the disk check after training), which FT1-F fixes before the GPU window. (4) AF-AP-187's class in prose: the ledger, the wiki and this log cited four commits by their LOCAL ids (e0d2bdc, 911a6cd, b97c84c, 2b2aa18), and `push_clean.sh` rewrote each on push (origin d03e7c4, ee09d94, f74dfd7, 3c9eb6f); corrected in these three files. A note written before a push cites the commit by its subject, or gets the origin id after the push.

**2026-09-24 10:5xZ — CI run #1026 red on a test race, not a product defect (AF-AP-181); found when push_clean's CI gate refused the acceptance push.** Run #1026 (eb71442, the 08:2xZ push) failed one of 3,902 tests: `tests/test_s0_01_frame_tee.py::TestSigtermTerminatesAgent::test_sigterm_kills_agent_child`, "agent pid 11674 state gone after 2 s, expected Z or gone". The test checked `os.path.exists("/proc/<pid>/stat")`, lost the read to the reaper, set `proc_state = "gone"` and asserted `proc_state == "Z"`; a second test in the same file (`test_sigterm_during_sha256_produces_status`) carried the same shape. Neither file is an attested input of S0-01 (its `result.json` attestation lists no `tests/` path), so the fix leaves every signed proof valid. Fix: one helper, `_proc_state_after_kill`, reads once and returns "gone" on any failed read; both sites assert `in ("Z", "gone")`. Regression: `TestProcStateAfterKill` (five tests: the lost read under ENOENT and ESRCH, a reaped pid, an exited unreaped child read as Z, a live pid read as neither, the state after the last paren). The class is registered as AF-AP-181 with a TEST_SCREEN signature and five fire/no-fire tests; the vendored manifest regenerated for the hook edit. Rule confirmed: the CI gate (AF-AP-126) did its job; a red run on the branch is read before any push, and "flake" is not a root cause.

**2026-09-24 07:5xZ — VERIFY-K215 home: K215 (task #215) and the anti-hollow-green bake (task #218) verified with follow-ups; the typed-escape quirk is wider than logged.** The sandbox Opus 5.5 verifier (task #219; `claude-opus-5-5` on 385 of 385 replies, no refusal stop) ran all twelve items at PIN bfe66ec and found no blocker: MERGE-READY-WITH-FOLLOWUPS. F-3's sharpest survivor: dropping the single quote from AF-AP-175's pattern loses 5 of 11 hits over `scripts/`, `harness-ports/`, `proofs/`, `src/` and `.github/` (for example `scripts/push_clean.sh:98`), while `tests/test_edit_snapshot_ap_screen.py` still reads `170 passed`. F-2 by reading: `_PathScoped.search` never matches, and both gates call `.search` without a path (`scripts/lint_delta.py:146`, `scripts/ap_screen.py:57`). - **What happened.** T94 (tasks #200 + #167) came home from the PC cloud build route at 18:2xZ with every gate green (T1 `67 passed, 0 failed`, T2 `pc_lane dispatcher: 42 passed, 0 failed`, run-all rc 0, six mutants killed). Its AF-AP-140 fix rewrote the poll probe in `scripts/pc_lane.sh` and escaped two PC-side lookups twice: `\\$(cat …/lane.pid)` and `\\$(find …/launch.log)`. Inside a double-quoted string, `\\$(` is a literal backslash followed by a command substitution that runs in the SANDBOX. Rendered with an echoing bridge, the PC would have received `kill -0 \ 2>/dev/null` and `[ -n \"\\" ]`; `bash -n` on the rendered probe: `unexpected EOF while looking for matching '"'` (rc 2). An empty probe matches no state, so every poll would have spun to MAX_POLLS and every lane would have read "timed out". The new FAILED predicate `(! test -f F -o F -ot B) && echo FAILED` was inverted too. Its truth table: a fresh marker gives no FAILED; a stale marker gives no FAILED; no marker and no brief gives FAILED.
- **How it was caught.** The coordinator's pre-commit read of the diff: the escaping changed on probe text the brief did not ask to change. Then the render through an echoing bridge, `bash -n`, and the four-case truth table.
- **Why the gates were green.** The dispatcher suite's fake bridge answers the probe from a `case` arm keyed on a substring and returns a canned state. It never runs the probe. The lane added an arm that matches its own new string, and its AF-AP-140 test pinned the probe's TEXT (`/FAILED -ot …/brief.md`) with no behavioral control (AF-AP-80). No test had ever run the poll probe, before T94 or after.
- **Fix (a coordinator amendment at the landing).** The single escaping is restored: the probe is byte-identical to the pre-T94 line except the FAILED predicate, now `test -f F && test ! F -ot B && echo FAILED`. Six new tests RUN the probe the dispatcher sends (`bash -c`, against a fake PC tree; `/fake` is rewritten only inside the bridge and exists nowhere locally, so a lookup expanded on the wrong side reads nothing): a stale FAILED with a live loop gives RUNNING; a fresh FAILED gives FAILED and rc 70; a report with a live loop gives RUNNING, never READY; a report with the loop gone gives READY; the launch window gives RUNNING; a stale launch.log gives GONE. Five mutants on scratch copies each red a named executed test. T2 `pc_lane dispatcher: 48 passed, 0 failed` twice; T1 `67 passed, 0 failed` twice; run-all rc 0.
- **Checked and not a defect.** A re-dispatch after an unattended FAILED reads the old marker as stale. That is correct: every dispatch sends the launch, the runner's state guard lets a new loop start when the old one is dead, and the new loop renames the old marker before it runs (`harness-ports/bin/pc-lane.sh`, the rename sits after the state guard).
- **Sweep (bug-echo, inline).** `grep -F '\\$('` over `scripts/`, `harness-ports/`, `proofs/`, `spikes/` and `src/`: no hit outside the two new comments. Remote programs in the dispatcher (a `\$` inside a `bridge "…"` string): three. The poll probe now runs in a test. The server-effort probe (`:292`, AF-AP-89's first instance) is canned-only, and the premise probe (`:211`) runs in the shared filesystem: both filed as issue #54.
- **Rule confirmed.** A test double for a remote command must RUN the command, against a fake remote root that differs from every local path. It must never answer from the command's text. A lane that adds a case arm for its own new string has written the test's answer. Registered as AF-AP-162.

Only T94 and S198A were sandbox tooling; J1-3 and J1-1-R3 were ordinary build lanes.

The cloud combo was read (read-only) before dispatch.

**2026-09-23 15:3xZ — A PC VERIFY LANE'S FAILED FINAL TURN CAME HOME AS ITS REPORT, WHILE ITS REAL 55 KB REPORT SAT UNHARVESTED IN THE LANE DIRECTORY (AF-AP-67, sixth family; task #200).**
- **What happened.** VERIFY-T92-T90R3-PCJ1 (PIN 433d15e, the local Qwen route, 153 API calls) wrote `VERIFY-T92-T90R3-PCJ1-report.md` (55,048 bytes, sha 8b3fb202…) into its lane directory at 16:33 PC local time (15:33Z). Its final turn then failed: Hermes printed `HTTP 400: [400]: No user query found in messages.` (T93's chat-template family) and wrote `usage.json` with `"completed": false, "failed": true`. The runner wrote that one line as `report.md`; the sandbox poller copied it to `tasks/briefs/pc/report-pc-verify-t92-t90r3-pcj1.md--433d15e.md` (50 bytes, sha 5ed8a086…) and printed `pc_lane: report ->`, then `no changes in the lane worktree` (the real report was outside the tree).
- **How it was caught.** The report's size (50 bytes) against the heartbeat's 49 KB draft. The coordinator listed the lane directory over the bridge, found the 55 KB report and `usage.json`, fetched the report in two base64 chunks (one base64 stream through `pc.sh` was corrupted, `base64: invalid input`), and verified the sha on both sides.
- **Rule confirmed.** AF-AP-67: a collector that keys on "a non-empty report" delivers a harness failure as the deliverable, and a denylist of failure texts grows one family per incident. This family (`HTTP 400: …`) matched none of `CAPACITY_RX`, `QUOTA_RX`, `SAFETY_RX`, `PERSIST_RX` or `^API call failed` (`harness-ports/bin/pc-lane.sh:269-305`). New here: the runtime had already said the session failed (`usage.json` `failed: true`), and nothing read it.
- **Fix.** Task #200: the runtime's own `failed`/`completed` flags decide FAILED before any text screen; a failed session never writes `report.md` from its last output; the brief-named report (in the tree or the lane directory) or the draft is promoted as PARTIAL with a header. Until it lands, the coordinator checks every harvested report's size against the heartbeat's draft and reads `usage.json` before grading.

**2026-09-23 14:5xZ — THE FIRST LIVE S0-02 CAPTURE CAUGHT A CONTAMINATED NEGATIVE LEG: THE BAD-SIGNATURE LEG SENDS A VALID PROBE INTO THE PROCESS IT OBSERVES (AF-AP-156, new; AF-AP-42 recurrence 4).**
- **What happened.** The seven owner-free legs ran on the PC from 14:37:08Z to 14:50:16Z, rc 0 (`~/s0-02-capture/c1`, runner head fa4532e). The coordinator pulled the bundle (sha256 aa4dde4b76386f79d174c3f90414ae56d5fa56f5c00c4c9aa1c188e0f6f63475, 48 files) and ran the committed checker on it in a scratch copy: `failure_reason: neg-bad-signature: 1 ACP session/prompt turn(s), expected 0`. The per-leg counts: pos-allowed 1 prompt (accepted 200); neg-unauthorized 0 (400 `restricted: not a channel member`); neg-bad-signature 1 (400 `invalid: invalid schnorr signature`); neg-replayed first 1 (accepted), second 0 (200 `duplicate:`); neg-stale 0 (400 `event timestamp too far from server time`); neg-self-authored 0 (accepted); neg-not-allowlisted 0 (accepted). Every leg matches its expected outcome except the bad-signature leg's turn count.
- **Mechanism.** `deliver_event.py` flips a signature only on a `--reuse` event, and it has no sign-only mode, so the runner's `neg-bad-signature)` branch (`proofs/S0-02/tools/pc/run_s0_02_legs.sh`, the `deliver pos-allowed "$out/.probe"` line) first POSTS a valid positive event to the relay inside the leg's own buzz-acp process, then reuses it with a flipped byte. The relay rejects the flipped copy for the right reason, but the probe's turn is already in the timeline, and `collect_leg` copies the whole timeline. The checker is right; the producer is wrong.
- **Why nothing caught it before.** B7's capture (2026-09-22) recorded only the relay receipt for this leg (`B7-report.md`: "http=400 accepted=False, invalid schnorr signature") and counted no prompts; no lane ran the checker over a live bundle; the committed synthetic bundle's bad-signature timeline holds zero prompts by construction, so the suite graded the checker against the ideal producer (AF-AP-42). T2 even pins the probe (`removed_nested == {".probe"}`).
- **Rejected fix.** Counting only the prompts after the flipped delivery's t0 in the checker: it would weaken the checker to fit a defective producer, and a real acceptance of a bad signature could then hide behind a probe's turn.
- **Fix (B12, task #196, after B11 lands because both hold `tests/test_s0_02_buzz_authz.py`).** `deliver_event.py` signs the template and flips one byte in ONE call, never posting the valid event; the runner's bad-signature branch makes exactly one delivery; the T2 probe pin becomes a no-probe pin; the contaminated live leg becomes the checker's regression input. Then all eight legs are captured again in one run (after the owner's removal of owner2), so one runner version produced the whole bundle.
- **Sweep (bug-echo, inline).** The seven PC runners were scanned for a negative leg whose setup performs the positive stimulus inside its observed window: S0-01's `negative` leg runs `pc_negative.py` alone; S0-03's credential-absent leg writes its own directory; S0-04's legs select their record by nonce after a sequence mark; S0-06's seed runs before the legs and each leg observes its own call. No other echo.

- **What happened.** At 12:34:49Z the lane's Bash call that wrote a test driver (`/tmp/ve3r1/drv/item2.py`) ended with `stop_reason: "refusal"` on `claude-opus-5-5`. The driver put executables named `ip` and `rm` first on PATH. Armed after the first signal, they held `ip netns del s0-05-hermes-acp` and `rm -f /run/s0-05-egress/<ns>.owner` for 2 s, to prove the S0-05 runner's cleanup survives a second signal inside cleanup. The harness dropped the lane's thinking blocks (a `thinking_drop` attachment, reason `model_mismatch`) and served the next 49 turns on `claude-opus-4-8` (12:34:55Z to 12:54:32Z). Nothing reached the coordinator, and the lane's draft header still read `claude-opus-5-5`. Found after the worker restart, by reading the served model per assistant record from the transcript (AF-AP-131's rule).

  - When only shims will do, expect the fallback, and make the report state its served-model mix.

The trigger class held three times: a test driver that PATH-shims `ip` or `rm` in a netns/egress cleanup test. The lane could not know the third refusal; the coordinator's note in the report states the mix, and the gates it rests on were reproduced (issue #48).

**2026-09-23 12:5xZ — A NEW BRIDGE BANNER, THEN A WORKER RESTART, EACH LEFT THE SURVIVING POLLERS ON A DEAD ENDPOINT (AF-AP-131's second and third instances; the 2026-09-22 18:3xZ entry's mechanism, measured).**
- **What happened.**
  - The owner re-ran the bridge (a new link, 12:5xZ). The coordinator rewrote `.pc-bridge.env` through the Write tool (never argv), moved the dead file out of the repo, and probed live.
  - Both PC-lane pollers kept looping on the old link (`no JSON envelope after retries`). `scripts/pc_bridge_exec.py` reads `PC_BRIDGE_URL`/`PC_BRIDGE_TOKEN` from its environment only, and that environment was set at launch. So they were re-attached.
  - Minutes later the session's worker process restarted. The `setsid` pollers SURVIVED it, but every call they made failed with `curl: (7) Failed to connect to 127.0.0.1 port 37899`. The agent proxy (`HTTPS_PROXY`) had moved to port 46507, and each process keeps the port it started with.
  - The monitor, a harness task, died with the worker.
- **Rule.** Two events orphan every long-lived sandbox poller and monitor without killing it: a new bridge banner (the link in the environment) and a worker restart (the proxy port in the environment). After either:
  1. Rewrite `.pc-bridge.env` if the banner changed, then probe with `scripts/pc.sh`.
  2. Re-attach each live lane from a CURRENT shell with the replay-safe `setsid nohup bash scripts/pc_lane.sh <brief> hermes <role>`. The log must say `premise gate skipped — a resume of <lane>`, and the PC lane pid must not change.
  3. Restart the monitor on the new logs.
  The stale pollers loop harmlessly until their poll ceiling, because an empty probe matches no case arm. A poller is alive only if its log shows a successful bridge call; `pgrep` does not prove it.
- This window's re-attaches: `lane-*-2.log` after the banner, `lane-*-3.log` after the restart. The PC lane pids stayed 3467866 (K1-h) and 3965057 (VERIFY-T92).

**2026-09-23 07:0xZ — THE S0-05 POSITIVE CONTROL ASKS A PATH THE REAL SERVICES REFUSE, AND ITS PREFLIGHT PASSES WHAT ITS CHECKER FAILS (AF-AP-139 registered; AF-AP-24 recurs).** Writing the E3-b brief, the coordinator measured the E3 report's open item from the PC host (read-only GETs): OmniRoute `:20128` answers `GET /v1/models` with 401 (the owner's key requirement) and the relay `:3999` with 404, while both answer 200 on a health path (`/api/health` JSON, `/health` text). The collector's C0 asks `/v1/models` and `check_egress.py` needs a 2xx, so both units would read `positive-control-failed` on the live leg. The runner's preflight says it checks "the exact predicate the proof consumes" (`run_s0_05_units.sh:354`) but passes any HTTP answer (`:358`), so it would launch a unit the checker then fails (AF-AP-24's shape: preflight and consumer diverge). E1, E2 and E3 all passed in the sandbox because every test stand-in answers 200 on every path: the request was never sent to the real services before the design froze (AF-AP-139). Nothing ran against the live leg, so nothing was lost; the fix is E3-b items P and R (health paths measured per service, recorded in the C0 record; the preflight grades the checker's predicate on the same request; a real-shaped stand-in as the negative control). The sweep of `tests/` and `harness-ports/tests/` for a repo-relative path in `spec_from_file_location` or a subprocess argv found no other instance: every other call site passes `ROOT / …` or an explicit `cwd=`. **2026-09-23 03:4xZ — A SHELL RANGE GUARD THAT PASSES THE VALUE IT CANNOT EVALUATE (AF-AP-129; found grading E2-R1).** E2-R1's up-front allow-entry validation (`proofs/S0-05/netns_lib.sh:77`, uncommitted lane output) reads `if ! [[ "$port" =~ ^[1-9][0-9]*$ ]] || [ "$port" -gt 65535 ]; then refuse`. A port of 20 digits matches the regex, and bash's `[` cannot hold it: it prints `integer expression expected` and returns 2, which the `||` reads as false, so the refusal never fires. Measured: `99999999999999999999` and `9223372036854775808` ACCEPTED, `65536` and `00080` refused. The entry then fails late, at `iptables --dport`, after the namespace exists: the "validate before any state" contract (F11/F12) is broken for that input class. The sweep of the 20 `[ "$var" -op N ]` guards under `proofs/ scripts/ harness-ports/bin/` found no other instance: every other guard either refuses on the error branch (`|| fail`, `|| die`) or tests an internal counter. Rule: a shell numeric test on input bounds the digit count first (or compares with `(( ))` after a length check); an error branch is never read as "in range". Tactic 1's NaN rule, in shell.

Coordinator side: the dispatcher will (T91) paste each lane’s MEASURED provider mix from `call_logs` into its harvest line, and the safety-block family goes to the FAILED regex (never a report, never a retry); VERIFY-GOV2c re-dispatches after the decision, or split under ~90k tokens so the compaction 400 never fires.

(4) AF-AP-105 SIBLING: `proofs/S0-01/tools/pc/run_leg.sh` (167e063, 2026-09-05) launched `pc_launch.py` detached into the fixed-name framedir `.markers/v2-<leg>` and polled `launch.ready` with only the launch LOG pre-removed — the exact shape registered on 2026-09-19 for S0-03's runner; found by reading the driver against the registry while authoring the brief (the mega-sweep rule: a fixed class has unexploded siblings). Fixed the same way (`rm -rf $FD` in the caller ahead of the launch) with a red-first structural test, plus a failure branch on the pre-manifest wait (a success-only poll). (5) SUBSTRATE BASELINE PINNED VOLATILE ENTRIES: the 2026-09-06 `manifest-baseline.txt.gz` carried 26 `__pycache__/*.pyc` entries of the pinned hermes-agent tree (written by the pre-flag exploratory runs); a foreign 2026-09-18 run (its producer is not in this repo) swapped that set (26 gone, 101 new) and left a root `.bytecode-fingerprint`; 24 of the 26 regenerated byte-identically, two did not (CPython 3.13.11 compiler output for two large modules), so byte-identity with the old baseline was unreachable and every leg would have failed `manifest body != baseline body` after ~10 minutes each — caught by the brief's P4 preflight (a manifest BEFORE any leg) at the cost of one 8-second manifest run. The tree cleanup was refused by the sandbox classifier and not worked around; the tree was re-pinned AS MEASURED (pins.py PINNED_BASELINE_* + the committed gz, the reason written into pins.py; buzz/acp/venv-hermes digests unchanged) — the caches and the marker are pinned entries, so a later foreign write fails loud. Rule: a substrate-identity baseline is re-verified BEFORE any long capture (a manifest costs seconds, a leg costs minutes), and a foreign writer into a pinned tree is a finding to name, not a drift to absorb. (6) RELAY MEDIA STORE OUTSIDE THE HARNESS: the throwaway `buzz-relay` of 2026-09-04 was launched without any `BUZZ_S3_*` setting, so its media/object store used the crate default `http://localhost:9000` (whatever served :9000 then — not the harness MinIO on :9471); after the 2026-09-21 reboot nothing served :9000 and the relay died at its start-up git object-store probe. The relaunch pins `BUZZ_S3_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:9471` + the harness bucket/creds — the isolation claim in GROUNDING.md ("own containers") was true for postgres/redis/minio and false for the relay's S3 target until now. Rule: a "throwaway stack" launch names EVERY backend explicitly — a crate default is a silent pointer into whatever else is running on the host. (7) THE GOLDEN DETERMINISM CLAIM FALSIFIED ON THE REAL AGENT (AF-AP-107): with a deterministic scripted backend, the pinned hermes-acp's `session_info_update` notification landed before the reply chunk in run-1, after the `end_turn` result in run-2, and between chunk and usage in both 2026-09-05 golden runs — three samples, three positions; the frozen checker's `check_golden` fails `golden mismatch between run-1 and run-2 at normalized line 7` (and `last normalized line is not end_turn terminal`), so the 2026-09-05 "golden x2 identical" was two matching coin tosses. NOT re-rolled (a re-run until two samples agree is a hollow green); every other check in the sequence passes on the real v2.4 bundle; the bundle is NOT minted; the golden's definition (order-free asynchronous notifications, N-sample multiset, or a documented limit) is an owner decision recorded in `tasks/briefs/s0-01-vb-f12-recapture-v24.md`. Rule: a golden is defined over the protocol-ordered sub-stream; unordered events are counted, never positioned; three samples before the word "deterministic". (8) COLLECT DIED ON A SIDECAR-LESS LEG (AF-AP-108): `collect_leg.sh`'s remote pack command ended on `[ -f manifest-<p>.txt.gz.sha256 ] && echo …`, so the negative leg (no manifests by contract) made the remote status 1, `pc.sh` propagated it, and `set -euo pipefail` killed the collect after the tar — the leg dir deleted, nothing fetched; the 2026-09-06 negative had reached the corpus through `realleg_sync.sh pc-build`, so the path had never run on such a leg. Fixed (`if … fi`) with an executable red-first control that runs the un-escaped loop in a bare directory. Rule: an optional-presence branch at the tail of a remote one-liner is an `if`, and the leg with the smallest file set is the test case for every "optional" branch. (9) THE RACE PROPAGATED INTO THE META-TEST FIXTURE + TWO CORPUS-VERSION ASSUMPTIONS: the synthetic passing bundle (`_session_bundle`) is built from the COMMITTED legs' directional frames, so once the real v2.4 legs replaced the withdrawn 2026-09-05 tree the synthetic run-2 inherited the real trailing `session_info_update` and 34 `bundle`-fixture tests were masked by the synthetic golden mismatch (both venues: `36 failed, 526 passed`); fixed in the fixture's own artifice (`_align_async_order`: run-2's asynchronous notifications take run-1's positions), `test_real_bundle_cli` a STRICT xfail carrying the AF-AP-107 reason and `test_real_bundle_fails_only_at_the_golden_race` pinning today's exact verdict; two `pc_tools` tests had hard-coded a v2.2 declared corpus (version-aware now). Rule: a fixture derived from committed evidence inherits every property of that evidence, including the ones under dispute — name the derivation and the property it neutralises; the three `tests/test_s0_01_spec_runner.py` deferral tests were the same class from the other side (they read the committed tree's v1 shape as their fixture and went red on the real bundle: `3 failed, 831 passed` in the eleven-file run) — `_strip_v2_evidence` now puts a COPY into the deferred state on purpose (`13 passed in 3.83s`), and the committed tree's real behaviour through the runner (`leg-exit-mismatch`, rc 1, no result.json) is pinned by its own test. (10) `*.pid` SWALLOWED A REQUIRED LEG FILE (an AF-AP-62 sibling): the five legs' `buzz-acp.pid` were gitignored — the working tree passed, a fresh clone would have failed `buzz-acp.pid absent`; re-included with `!proofs/*/evidence/**/buzz-acp.pid`, and the sweep `git ls-files -o --ignored --exclude-standard <evidence root>` = 0 is now a step before every evidence commit. (11) TWO LANE-PROCESS WRINKLES reported by VB-F12-T2 (the first VB-F12 follow-up on the local-Qwen build lane: 46 min, one file, MERGE-READY proposal, harvested 15:2xZ): a persistent-cwd `terminal` tool made a later relative-path read hit the SCRATCH copy of `scripts/proof-runner` instead of the tree (stale-sink; guard: absolute-path roots in any call that reads source for a cited line — the lane re-derived every line claim from the tree), and backticking `report_lint`'s own summary inside the report turned its digits into new refs, so each re-run changed the count it measured (a self-referential measure; guard: paste a linter's numeric summary as PLAIN text; tooling follow-up: `report_lint.py` should ignore its own summary shape). No source defect; no registry row — neither has a mechanical signature in project code. (12) VB-F12-G1 (the golden decision (a), the local-Qwen build lane, 3 h 55 m, MERGE-READY-WITH-FOLLOWUPS proposal): FOUR brief-authoring defects surfaced by the lane, all the coordinator's — (a) the brief's item 6 asserted the PC corpus "still carries the OLD golden.jsonl": it carries NO golden.jsonl at all (`realleg_sync.sh` copies leg directories only) and no `test_real_leg_*` reads the pin — an environmental premise stated from memory, not measured (AF-AP-4's cousin: a brief premise is a claim; measure it at authoring); (b) the gate instruction inherited from A5p ("`scripts/pc_suite.sh launch … wait`") cannot run ON the PC — pc_suite is the sandbox's bridge launcher (`bridge_http_code=000` for the lane); a PC-resident lane runs `pytest -n 8` directly (xdist is installed there); (c) C6(i)'s literal mutation ("the first agent_message_chunk moved before the session/prompt request") is structurally unreachable — `check_prompt_turn` (C:1826) rejects it before `check_golden` (C:1856): an evidence mutation must target the FIRST check that sees it, or it tests check ORDER, not the check; (d) the checker's PASS line reads `negative: observed: observed: error code=…` (a doubled prefix, cosmetic; every consumer matches by substring — a `verify-followup` issue, not a round). Plus two coordinator process quirks baked into CLAUDE.md: a trailing `&` backgrounds a whole `&&` list (the list's own `rm -f pidfile` raced the pid write), and a poller relaunched inside a Monitor script dies with the monitor's expiry (`setsid nohup … </dev/null &`, then find the real pid by pgrep — `$!` is setsid's short-lived parent). (13) THE MINT ITSELF BROKE TWO DEFERRAL TESTS (the PC 13-file gate on e458801: `2 failed, 1401 passed in 142.15s`): `test_positive_leg_defers_on_v1_evidence` and `test_runner_defers_s0_01_via_real_runner` copy `proofs/` (now carrying the committed `proofs/S0-01/result.json`), strip the v2 evidence, run the deferral and assert `result.json` is ABSENT — the runner PRESERVES an existing artifact on deferral ("artifact preserved"), so the copied mint made the assertion false; and `test_committed_bundle_runner_mints_a_result` (T2/G1) asserted `result.is_file()` on a copy that already carried it — a hollow green from the same inheritance. Fix: `_strip_v2_evidence` unlinks the copied artifact (the deferred state never had one; the tests prove NON-CREATION) and the mint test unlinks before running (proves CREATION). The class is item 9's again: a fixture copied from the committed tree inherits every property of it, including the artifact a later increment adds — a test over a copied tree must strip what its premise excludes, and a "file exists after the run" assertion must delete the file first. The lane's two-file gate could not see it (S passed there because the lane tree had no result.json); the coordinator's 13-file PC gate on the pushed mint did — the reason the gate runs on the pushed head after every mint. (14) VERIFY-VB-F12 (the local-Qwen verify lane, ~1 h 15 m, NOT-READY): F1 BLOCKER — G1's two negative controls (5c the synchronous-order control, 5d the closed-set control) built their "moved" list with `entries[idx+1:]` where `entries[idx+2:]` was needed, DUPLICATING the neighbour: the output differed because a frame was ADDED, so M4 (the async tuple widened to `tool_call`) and M8 (the async sort removed) survived the whole suite while the real checker's verdicts changed (M4 passes a forbidden tool_call reorder; M8 fails a valid two-async opposite-order capture) → AF-AP-109; repair round G2 on the Qwen build lane (T only; the normalizer is correct — the mutants change its behaviour, only the tests did not bind it). SOLID elsewhere: the decision's reach (count, payload, session bound; only the designated kind's raw position freed), the bundle's manifests/receipts/negative identity, the mint's attested outputs byte-faithful, ledger-gen byte-identical. F2 the doubled `negative: observed: observed:` prefix (C:1556 wraps what negative_contract already prefixed; C:1876 prefixes again; no consumer parses the PASS line) → verify-followup issue. F3 decision (a) had no D-row → D-035. The MINT stands; ACCEPTANCE waits for G2 + a re-verify of F1.





**2026-09-18 — S0-03 leg B PROVEN LIVE (a real Hermes tool-call round trip through real OmniRoute); the 4+-recapture blocker was a provider-selector prefix leaking to the aggregator, not a credential failure (AF-AP-103).** The leg-B blocker that survived five config/credential shapes (bare provider; `custom:` + key_env; provider `base_url`; S0-01's exact structure; an `auth.json` credential pool) all 401'ing `No active credentials for provider: custom:s0-03-omniroute` was NOT a Hermes credential problem: two in-process probes showed `resolve_runtime_provider()` (the exact function `acp_adapter/auth.py:22` calls) resolves the api_key cleanly for every shape. The 401 is OMNIROUTE's — Hermes forwards `model.default` verbatim as the wire model id, and `model.default: custom:s0-03-omniroute/agentfactory-build` carried the Hermes-internal provider prefix; OmniRoute read `custom:s0-03-omniroute` as a provider it has no connection for. A direct 3-way `chat/completions` probe isolated it: `model=agentfactory-build` → 200 (real gpt-5.6-sol-ultra), `model=custom:s0-03-omniroute/agentfactory-build` → the exact 401, `model=custom:s0-03-omniroute` → 400 "Unable to determine provider". Fix: `model.default: agentfactory-build` (bare combo id) + `model.provider: custom:s0-03-omniroute` (the `custom:` prefix stays where Hermes reads the provider). Proven live: leg B is now 33 frames — `tool_call: 2` (`terminal: printf <nonce>` → completed), `agent_message_chunk: 10`, the agent's final message is the nonce — a real tool-call round trip. Blocker 2 (the call_logs binding) is also fully characterised against the real rows: 3 leg-B rows carry `session_tag == the injected nonce` + `combo_name=agentfactory-build`, `status=200`, `provider=codex`, `requested_model=codex/gpt-5.6-sol-ultra`, `response_id` empty (dead). Rules confirmed: build-loop 4 ("the live run is the real proof") — the bug survived O2/O3 build+verify rounds because leg B had never run live; deep-work Phase 0 (a source-read is a hypothesis — the credential theory read from source was falsified TWICE before the direct aggregator probe settled it). Registry AF-AP-103; AF-AP-100/101 CONFIRMED, AF-AP-102's own correction superseded. The call_logs realign LANDED (lane O4, commit `be083e3`, 193 passed ×2 sandbox, 6 hostile bundles red-then-green). But the FIRST live re-capture with the realigned tooling caught a SECOND fixtures-as-mirror layer (AF-AP-101 recurring): the round-trip conjunct (iv) + the runner's AF-AP-100 assertion were built from a FICTIONAL ACP `tool_call` shape — the real one has `content` as a LIST of blocks + a top-level `title`, and **no `rawInput`**, so the runner crashed (`'list' object has no attribute 'get'`, run_s0_03_legs.sh:256 `content.get("title")`) and the checker's `rawInput.command` bind (check_roundtrip:674) can never match a real tool_call. The real tool_call golden shape + the fix (bind the execute tool_call by title/content command + toolCallId, then the same-id completed update) are recorded in `proofs/S0-03/LIVE-CAPTURE-FINDINGS.md` §"Blocker 3". The round-trip-shape fix (runner + checker + fixtures' timeline.jsonl) + re-capture + mint is the follow-on. Lesson: "pin a real row" applies to EVERY external shape a checker parses — the call_logs rows AND the ACP timeline frames; the O4 brief pinned the former, not the latter.

**2026-09-18 — S0-08 gVisor containment PROVEN LIVE on the PC; the pinned Hermes image built with rootless BuildKit after three build-environment findings; the runner's first real podman run exposed a latent image-id format bug.** The owner asked the coordinator to drive S0-08 (gVisor being fully unblocked with zero owner dependency). The pinned Hermes image (`git archive` of the pin `527da608`, unmodified Dockerfile) would not build under `run_containment.sh`'s default `podman build`: line 286's BuildKit symbolic `COPY --link --chmod=a+rX,go-w . .` is rejected by podman/buildah AND buildkit's built-in `dockerfile.v0` frontend, both octal-chmod-only (AF-AP-97) — built via standalone rootless BuildKit (`rootlesskit buildkitd`, single-level rootless, no daemon/sudo) with the EXTERNAL `docker/dockerfile:1` frontend selected at invocation (gateway.v0), pinned Dockerfile untouched, `HERMES_GIT_SHA` build-arg → baked provenance `revision == 527da608` verified. The build stalled 30 s at `npx playwright install chromium`: the PC's only global IPv6 route is a black-holing Tailscale ULA (`fd7a:…`, pref low), node's downloader prefers the AAAA and blocks while a host `curl -L` uses IPv4 (AF-AP-98) — fixed with an IPv4-only `slirp4netns` netns. The reproducible build is committed as `proofs/S0-08/tools/pc/build_image_buildkit.sh`. `run_containment.sh --image` then ran the image under runsc (release-20260817.0, sha 048b89aa…) and exited 2 on its image-identity check: podman's `{{.Id}}`/`{{.Image}}` are bare hex, the check required the docker `sha256:` prefix — a latent bug from a runner never PC-run (AF-AP-99); fixed by normalizing the producer to the canonical `sha256:` form (oracle untouched; 145 S0-08 tests green). The re-run captured a real evidence bundle (`proofs/S0-08/evidence/pc-runsc/`): `check_containment.py` PASS (6 properties), P1 `uname -r=4.19.0-gvisor` + dmesg "Starting gVisor" (host is 6.17.11), P3 real Hermes v0.21.0/python/node/uv inside, P5 host sentinel unreadable, P6 `unshare --net`/mount blocked; both negative controls fail as specified. Good-state reached (headline proven, gate green). The formal mint (registry class-flip blocked_host→execution_proof per the runsc positive transition, remove `blocked.json`, `result.json`, attested-input regen, the four blocked-assumption tests) is the follow-up increment.


**2026-09-07 — the code-intel instruments were not run on the S0-01 verification loops (owner escalation): graft's MCP server timed out at session start, code-review-graph's graph was never built, ripwire / sentrux / slopo never ran on a lane delta, the anti-pattern screen only ever saw edited hunks.** Root causes, measured: (1) graft answers `initialize` in 0.5 s on an idle box and 1.0 s during a concurrent build — the 34 s CONNECT_TIMEOUT at 12:37Z was the fresh container's startup stampede (npm/pip installs + GitNexus analyze + graft build on 4 cores); setup's background builds are now delayed and niced, and the durable fix is `MCP_TIMEOUT=120000` in the CCR environment's env vars (owner step); a failed server never reconnects mid-session — the CLI is the same-session path. (2) code-review-graph's index was "lazy — first use" and no first use ever came; setup now builds it in the background (78 s). (3) nothing on the loop's path INVOKED the instruments: `scripts/lane_context.sh` is now the one-command pass every brief attaches, `scripts/report_lint.py` and `scripts/ap_screen.py` gate every checkpoint. Rule confirmed the hard way: an instrument that is not in a script on the path is not in the loop — "use it religiously" without a mechanical trigger decayed within a day.

**2026-09-07 — a build lane BACKGROUNDED a mutant run and stopped; the run wedged in a mutual pipe deadlock for three hours (VERIFY-B5g F-B5g-9).** Lane B5g ran its PIDFILE-ABSENT mutant as a background job, reported "post-suite census: live leaks: 0" from a census scoped to its foreground suite, and returned; the backgrounded pytest (the three grandchild tests) and its `frame_tee.py` child sat blocked on each other — the test held the tee's stdin write end while blocking on the tee's stdout, the tee's c2a reader blocked on that stdin — until the verifier found the pair by `wchan` + the shared pipe inode in both fd tables, and the coordinator killed it after `/proc` identity checks (cwd under the lane's scratch venue, PID-targeted). Rules confirmed: long gates in ONE foreground call (a backgrounded run is never rewoken and never censused); a report's process census is OWN-VENUE-scoped and PASTED, never asserted. Fix in flight: lane B5h bounds every read of the tee's output and closes the tee's stdin before any wait; the deadlock class gets its registry row when the root cause lands.

**2026-09-07 — the day's ledger, wiki and task-DB stamps drifted up to 2.8 h ahead of the commit clock (checkpoint 8p stamped 19:0xZ, committed 16:29Z; VERIFY-B5f stamped 21:0xZ, committed 18:15Z).** The coordinator typed every `HH:MxZ` stamp from a running mental clock instead of pasting one; the drift grew through the day, and the task DB copied it. Rule confirmed: AF-AP-37 (typed numbers) covers TIMESTAMPS — a stamp is pasted from `date -u +%H:%MZ` or from the commit clock (`git log -1 --date=format:%H:%MZ --format=%cd <sha>`), never typed. Fix: every 2026-09-07 stamp since 8n re-derived from the commit clock in the same sync; build-loop rule 2 and the CLAUDE.md index line extended.

**2026-09-07 — the PC checker gate went red on a world-scoped scan test under 8 xdist workers (AF-AP-59, AF-AP-4's parallel cousin).** `tests/test_s0_01_pc_post_scan.py::test_teardown_scan_names_an_owned_survivor_whatever_its_command` asserted `rows == [agent]` on the teardown scan; PC run 20260907T161133Z (worker gw6) saw a second row: pid 985252, ppid 982853 (ANOTHER worker's process), command `python -c import time; time.sleep(120) # …/frame_tee.py` — a sibling test's pinned-path sleeper. The producer lists every owned row AND every live row naming a pinned path, whoever spawned it (`pc_post.sh:67`), so the body is world-scoped by design; the test assumed an empty world, which is only true serially (the sandbox, and the first PC run of the day by luck). Four assertions in the file carried the assumption (`pinned_present == 0`, three exact row sets). Fix: `_split_world(rows, owned)` — assert the OWNED subset exactly and characterise every foreign row (it must name a pinned path — the producer's only other admission rule; anything else fails as unexplained); negative control run (an unexplained `sleep 60` row rejected, a pinned foreign row accepted). Rule: a test over a system-wide enumeration (ps, /proc, ports, files) asserts its own subset exactly and the admissibility of the rest, never emptiness; the two-venue discipline (serial sandbox AND parallel PC) is what caught it.

**2026-09-07 — a verifier-proposed mutant killer let the very mutant it named live (VERIFY-N5g-b F1, probe test).** The verifier's non-blocking F1 asked the post-loop killer to print its own mechanism (`RL_CALLS=2 DEADLINE_SEEN=0.0`) so an inlined early-loop deadline (mutant DL-INLINE) would die. Landed as written and RUN against the mutant: the mutant SURVIVED (`1 passed`) — the fixture's gate was ORDINAL ("call 1 fails, call 2 succeeds"), so under a real 0.2 s deadline the early loop's second attempt succeeded 2 ms later with the identical error text and the count still read 2. Fix: a PHASE gate — every child readlink fails while the main thread is alone (`threading.active_count() == 1`, i.e. before the stderr drain thread the probe starts right after the early loop); the fake path is served only at the post-loop site. DL-INLINE now retries ~100× → `RL_CALLS≠2` → red (proven: clean `1 passed` / mutant `1 failed` on the RL_CALLS line, probe restored byte-identical). Rules confirmed: AF-AP-36 (a reviewer-named mutant becomes a committed failing test — RUN, never reasoned; a verifier's proposed killer is a hypothesis like any other claim) and anti-hollow-green tactic 3 (new 3d: gate fakes on PHASE/state, never on call ordinals — the ordinal is the first thing a mutant shifts). Echo sweep (bug-echo, literal): no other ordinal gates in `tests/` `proofs/` `spikes/` (`_calls[0] ==` / `call_count ==` / `attempts[0] ==` — 0 hits).

**2026-09-07 — lane B5e's F13 "handler before Popen" was a half fix that turned a clean rc -15 into a traceback (frame tee).** The SIGTERM handler moved to `frame_tee.py:123` but the `try:` that catches `_Terminated` stayed at `:413` — a TERM anywhere in :123-:413 (Popen, the entrypoint sha256 read, identity write, state build, initial status) raises `_Terminated` outside any try: traceback, rc 1, NO status file. The new docstring claimed "still produces rc 70 and a status file" and the lane's fixed-delay 200 ms test landed after :413 on a small agent file — green for the wrong reason. Reproduced twice with a 234 MB entrypoint (sha256 ≈ 0.5 s): TERM at 51 ms and at 68 ms (the instant `pgrep -P <tee>` showed the child) → `rc=1 status_present=False`, stderr tail `raise _Terminated()`. Fix design (follow-up brief to B5e): everything the except path needs defined BEFORE the install, `proc=None` pre-init, ONE try wrapping everything after the install, a deterministic window test (big entrypoint + child-exists poll) with its red-state line, an AST pin that nothing sits between the install and the try, an honest docstring (only interpreter startup before the install stays uncovered: rc -15, no status). Rules confirmed: build-loop 4 (prove the fix at the boundary where the failure was observed — the lane's test never reached the window it named) and the hollow-green-in-prose rule. Echo sweep (bug-echo, literal `signal.signal(` in project code): the checker's SIGALRM install (`check_acp_conformance.py:1587`) also precedes its try, but its handler raises `SystemExit(70)` with the reason already printed — the effect does not depend on the except path, and alarm granularity (≥ 1 s) makes the microsecond gap unreachable: not an echo.

**2026-09-06 — checkpoint 8k's PC gate red on a fixture that assumed `/bin/dash` (AF-AP-4 recurrence).** Lane N5g's new `test_probe_interpreter_deleted_after_start_is_a_loud_probe_error` copied `/bin/dash` as the agent's private interpreter; the sandbox (Debian) and CI (Ubuntu) have it, the owner's Fedora PC does not → `1 failed, 288 passed` on the venv leg and `1 failed, 105 passed` on the 3.13 leg, `FileNotFoundError: '/bin/dash'`. The docstring also called the copy a hardlink (it never was; a hardlink into tmpfs would fail with EXDEV on the PC anyway). Fix: the fixture copies `os.path.realpath("/bin/sh")` (dash here, bash there; the agent script is POSIX `exec 1>&-; sleep`), docstring corrected — green on all three legs. Rule: a fixture never names a distro-specific binary; resolve the system shell/interpreter at test time, and the PC leg runs BEFORE a checkpoint is called gated.

**2026-09-06 — a SINGLE Kimi K3 lane on the PC still died on the exhausted codex fallback chain (lane A5g, 17:5x-18:2xZ).** Dispatched with an explicit `HERMES_MODEL=ollama-cloud/kimi-k3` after a 24-token probe that served `kimi-k3`, as PC-BRIDGE.md rules, and with no other Ollama-Cloud lane running (the two other `hermes` processes on the PC are the owner's own sessions, 35 h and 24 days old). ~30 min in, the lane's only log line is `API call failed after 3 retries: HTTP 429: [codex/gpt-5.5-xhigh] All codex accounts have exhausted their quota (reset after 27h 45m 26s)` — i.e. Hermes walked the profile's codex fallback chain regardless of the pinned model (the Kimi-side trigger is not in the 100-byte log; the cooldown class is the likely cause under Hermes' request rate). The lane got far (3 files, +1055/−140) and the partial is kept as optional reference (`tasks/briefs/s0-01-a5g-support/attempt1-partial.diff`), the lane relaunched in the sandbox on Opus 4.6. Rule (PC-BRIDGE.md): until the codex quota resets (~2026-09-07 22:00Z) the PC Hermes lane is NOT a build venue — `HERMES_MODEL` does not stop the fallback walk, and one Kimi lane is enough to trip it; the PC stays the SUITE venue (`pc_suite.sh`). Open item for the owner: a lane profile with NO fallback chain (Kimi-only), so a 429 fails the call instead of burning the codex quota path.

**2026-09-06 — a schema edit invalidated the four minted proofs: CI runs 106-110 red on `attestation-mismatch`.** Lane N5f added a `pattern` to `failure_reason` in `proofs/schemas/spec.schema.json` (VERIFY-N5e F9). Every minted `result.json` (S0-09, S0-10, S0-11, S0-12) carries an ATTESTATION block hashing its tooling inputs — `scripts/proof-runner`, `scripts/validate-ledger`, `proofs/registry.yaml`, the five schemas, the proof's own files — so `validate-ledger integrity` flipped all four to INVALID and CI's stage1-gate, ledger-integrity and tests jobs went red at checkpoint 8j (`4 failed, 1246 passed, 64 skipped, 1 xfailed`). This is the neuter-the-tooling-keep-the-green defence working as built, not a defect in the schema change; what was missing was the regeneration step in the same increment and an `integrity` check in the coordinator gate for any change to an attested path. Fix: the four artifacts regenerated (`python3 scripts/proof-runner run --proof <id> --venue sandbox --root .`) — the diffs are the schema hash, the volatile timestamps and the result digests; the NORMALIZED digests MOVED as well — the attestation block is substantive by design (normalization.yaml exempts only `recorded_at`, `env_fingerprint`, the per-run timestamps and the raw `digest`) — a fact the first commit message (f1e1316) got wrong: it said "normalized digests unchanged", a claim typed from the CI diff on the OLD artifacts, and the chain's diff guard let it through because `grep 'digest'` also matches `normalized_digest` (AF-AP-37 recurrence: a claim about a diff is pasted from THAT diff, and a guard grep anchors the exact key). No document cites the old digests; S0-11's ACCEPTED status is bound by the status line + task row (check-proof-status), not by a digest — its regenerated artifact is check-identical, only the attestation and timestamps differ, recorded here for the owner; `validate-ledger integrity` PRESENT ×4 with the four-way denominators restored (`conformance_checked_decision 3/3`, `execution_proof 1/7`); `ledger-gen` re-run and committed. Rule (CLAUDE.md, registry AF-AP-56): a change under `proofs/schemas/`, to `scripts/proof-runner` / `scripts/validate-ledger` / `proofs/registry.yaml`, or to a minted proof's own files is a TOOLING change — regenerate every dependent artifact in the same increment and gate on `validate-ledger integrity` + `ledger-gen` + `git diff --exit-code proofs/ledger.json`; a lane whose boundary contains an attested path carries that gate in its brief. Both venue gates (sandbox, PC) were blind because neither ran the ledger integrity check.

**2026-09-06 — the lanes-live push left the branch ref behind; VERIFY-N5e finds the probe sampling an INTERMEDIATE exec stage.** (1) The first `push_clean.sh --lanes-live` push (checkpoints 8f-8g) rewrote and pushed from a detached worktree: origin took `220ffde`, the local branch ref stayed at the pre-rewrite `152d023` — three commits apart, IDENTICAL trees (`git diff` empty; `rev-list --left-right` 3/3). Reconciled by `git update-ref` after proving tree identity; fix `c718d25`: the mode follows origin only when the branch tree equals origin's and warns without moving otherwise; exercised by the next push (`303600a`, ref followed). Rule: a divergence over an identical tree after a copy-side rewrite is THIS class, never a parallel session. Registry AF-AP-54 (a git-ops class with no Python signature — no AP_SCREEN row). (2) VERIFY-N5e (round 9): the N5e early interpreter sample accepts the FIRST `/proc/<pid>/exe` reading; for `#!/usr/bin/env python3` and sh-wrapper agents that is `/usr/bin/env` / `/usr/bin/dash`, silently and run-to-run variable (9/12, 3/3), while the parent commit read python 12/12 — a REGRESSION minted by a lane whose fixtures were all single-exec (`#!{sys.executable}`), so the suite was blind to the exec-stage class; plus the dead `_last_good` arm claimed as built in the commit, comment and report (delete-mutant equivalent; a raise-instrument never fired) and the M3 crash path's partial evidence set (`env.json absent` reported instead of the producer crash). Rulings in `tasks/briefs/s0-01-n5f-probe-later-reading-wins.md`; lesson baked into `anti-hollow-green` (a sample of a process's identity is a sample of a STAGE — pin the reading to the first event only the final stage can produce; every identity suite carries a multi-stage-exec fixture) and the edit-snapshot screen; registry AF-AP-55.

**2026-09-06 — a depth bomb that only the CI interpreter lets through, and the Ollama Cloud cooldown under two Kimi lanes.** (1) CI runs 87-89 (three identical-code heads, Python 3.12) failed lane D5e's `test_depth_1000_json_body_returns_400_no_record` with an EMPTY response: CPython's C JSON decoder accepts depth 1000 on 3.12/3.13 (only 3.11 — the sandbox venv AND the PC venv — raises RecursionError inside `json.loads`), so the body parsed and the handler died later in the record serializer with no bytes sent — the fail-open crash path VERIFY-D5d F10 had named, opened by the interpreter rather than the walk. Reproduced on the PC under 3.13 at the pushed tip (`1 failed`). Fix `3c9a172`: the bound comes from the BYTES — `_json_nesting_depth` (escape-aware linear scan outside strings) and `MAX_JSON_DEPTH = 32` refuse the body before any parse (400 + close, no record); exact-bound tests (MAX+1 → 400, red on a gate-removed copy; MAX → 200 + one record); PC venv `437 passed in 52.33s`; PC Python 3.13 `3 passed`; CI run 94 SUCCESS. Registry: AF-AP-50 (a bound that comes from the interpreter's recursion behaviour is venue-dependent — bound inputs by scanning the bytes; every "must raise" fixture needs a run on the CI interpreter or a venue-independent mechanism). Both gate venues run 3.11 while CI runs 3.12 — the PC's system python (3.13, pytest 9.0.2) is the cheap extra leg for interpreter-sensitive tests. (2) Lane A5f, dispatched on the PC/Kimi 60 s after N5e, died on attempt 1: `HTTP 429 … codex/gpt-5.5-xhigh` — two concurrent Kimi lanes tripped the Ollama Cloud per-credential cooldown, Hermes walked the profile's codex fallback chain (every member `exhausted their quota`), and the PC-side `pc-lane.sh` retried only the 503 signature. Fix `ff6e492`: the 429 cooldown joins the retry class, the quota 429 is excluded (`QUOTA_RX`), two tests (`35 passed, 0 failed`). The PC clone was still at checkpoint 5's transcripts sync (ec0eee7) — the PC-side script in use was that old version; ff-synced to 151e524. Rule (PC-BRIDGE.md): ONE Ollama-Cloud lane at a time; the dead lane dir moved aside (`.failed-429-1`); A5f relaunches when N5e lands.

**2026-09-06 — the first Kimi K3 build lane on the PC, the push blocked by a live lane, and two tool quirks.** (1) Kimi K3's Ollama-Cloud cooldown lifted at 12:34Z (probe 200, served=kimi-k3); per the owner's instruction lane D5e ran on the PC Hermes lane with `HERMES_MODEL=ollama-cloud/kimi-k3` set explicitly (never the combo, which degrades to `big-pickle`) after a 24-token pre-dispatch probe; Hermes' `session_model_usage` billed the session to kimi-k3; the lane finished in ~15 min with a real report, reproduced the leak before touching code, flagged one deviation loud (`unquote_plus` had to join the closure for the `+` separator), and its patch passed the coordinator's PC gate `435 passed in 52.25s` and the sandbox red file `47 passed` — quality graded by VERIFY-D5e. N5e and A5f followed on the same route (two lanes = the admission cap). (2) `push_clean.sh` refuses a dirty tree while a sandbox lane edits it, so checkpoints 8b-8d sat unpushed for ~1 h 20 min; that blocked lane A5e's PC gate (the PC clone lacked the unpushed HEAD). Rule: a sandbox build lane in the shared tree defers every push until it lands; `scripts/pc_suite.sh` now takes `PC_SUITE_BASE=<pushed sha>` so a PC gate can run on an unpushed head (patch = the unpushed commits + the working tree). Prefer PC lanes (worktrees on the PC) whenever a sanctioned model answers — they never dirty the shared tree. (3) Quirks: a commit message typed inline in double quotes had its backtick spans EXECUTED by the shell (`62 passed…` → `62: command not found`, the pasted counts vanished; amended) — write the message to a file with a quoted heredoc and pass `-m "$(cat file)"`; `safe_commit.sh` refuses when the index already carries staged entries, so a lane patch applied with `git apply --index` must be unstaged (`git restore --staged`) before safe_commit re-stages the named paths. (4) Verify verdicts landed this stretch: VERIFY-D5d NOT-READY (a live leak via encoded-whitespace splits — the screen composed strip∘unquote only; the red-file oracle mirrored it; three untested new paths) → D5e closed it with closure semantics; VERIFY-N5d NOT-READY on three ≤10-line mechanical blockers with all four round-7 blockers closed → N5e. Registry: AF-AP-49 (normalization applied in ONE order mirrors the attacker's chosen order; a screen must test membership under the closure of its normalizations, and its test oracle must be a strictly wider, independent lattice).

**2026-09-06 — the owner's model route ran dry mid-wave; a combo that degrades silently; three lanes fell back to the sandbox; the N5c verifier caught a hollow spot in the coordinator's own test.** (1) 10:42Z lane D5d on the PC died with `HTTP 429: [codex/gpt-5.5-xhigh] All codex accounts have exhausted their quota (reset after 35h 20m)`; A5d's four attempts had been refused (503 admission, the precursor); B5c died later (harness rc=137) with only its premise draft. Probing OmniRoute from the PC with the lane profile's own key: `codex/gpt-5.6-sol-ultra` 429 (quota), `ollama-cloud/kimi-k3` and `glm-5.2` 429 (`credentials … cooling down`, still at 11:24Z), and the `agentfactory-build` COMBO answered 200 in 1 s served by `big-pickle` — the `free-coding` tail. Hermes' `session_model_usage` shows the B5c session took one `big-pickle` response (3192 output tokens): part of its diff is free-tier output. Rules: a build lane is dispatched with an explicit `HERMES_MODEL` from the sanctioned set (codex sol/terra, kimi-k3, glm-5.2) after a 24-token probe records the SERVED model — never the combo while a member is out; recommend dropping `free-coding` from the build combo so exhaustion fails loud (a build lane must never degrade silently). The owner's standing instruction (10:5xZ): use Kimi K3 on Ollama Cloud when OpenAI is out — honoured as soon as its cooldown lifts. (2) The sandbox Opus 4.6 fallback ran D5d (DONE, verify pending), A5d (PARTIAL: 16 rules, 21 guards still untested → A5e) and takes N5d and B5c next; suites for the checker run on the PC through `scripts/pc_suite.sh` (no model needed). (3) VERIFY-N5c NOT-READY: the probe exits 0 with an unsampled interpreter identity on the pinned agent's normal answer-then-exit shape (R7-N5c-F1), SR-03 is a real behavioural difference whose designated killer test is a tautology in both builds (F2), two tests encode the sandbox coincidence that the runner and the fixture shebang resolve to one interpreter (F4/F8 — the PC reds), and the coordinator's producer-pin test asserted a 7-way reason set where the reason is deterministic (F5 — a hollow spot in a test written to close AF-AP-42; `assert x in {candidates}` is the set-shaped sibling of `ok or <substring>`, registered as AF-AP-48). Also F11: the checkpoint-7 commit pasted the lane's pre-integration `227 passed` where the integrated tree yields 228 — AF-AP-37 recurrence; paste the post-integration run. (4) Guard-condition lesson: the relaunched verifier's prompt said STOP if the brief's last line ends with X, meaning STOP unless it does — the verifier reasoned the antecedent false and proceeded; state guard conditions positively ("the file must end with X; otherwise STOP").

**2026-09-06 — container restart mid-wave, the CI read for checkpoint 7, and the PC-lane admission cap.** (1) The sandbox container restarted at ~10:30Z with five background tasks live (the B5c/D5d harvesters, the N5c verifier, two wake timers); the coordinator's context rolled back to before its last commit (`4b173b7`, the producer `ps -eww` fix) while the workspace and origin carried it — the three-clock compare (origin tip `cc03846` = local; chat digest last entry 10:24Z; commit 10:26Z) resolved it in one step: origin ahead of memory was my own later work, exactly as the session-continuity rule predicts. The PC lanes were unaffected (detached on the PC; `scripts/pc_lane.sh` re-attaches through its PC-side guard — pidfile alive → never a second lane, report present → re-print). The N5c verifier died with an empty transcript and was relaunched fresh with a premise update. (2) CI read for checkpoint 7 (`73d025d`, run 79): `1 failed, 1037 passed, 64 skipped, 10 xfailed in 682.87s` — the one red is `test_agent_exits_without_consuming_stdin_exit_70` (`assert 0 == 70`), the race-dependent tee test VERIFY-B5b F4 named, now red on two consecutive CI runs (7cd83c7, 73d025d) and green on 5f81e34; lane B5c's ruling applies (the 712fe01 pattern). The ten xfails are the committed D5c red file holding. (3) Three PC Hermes lanes dispatched 90 s apart: the third (A5d) was refused on four attempts with `HTTP 503: Chat admission capacity is temporarily unavailable` while B5c and D5d ran — the owner's model route admits about two concurrent lane sessions. Rule (PC-BRIDGE.md): at most TWO concurrent PC lanes; queue the rest and launch each when a report lands. `pc-lane.sh`'s own retry (3 retries, 120/240 s backoff) cannot bridge an hour of both slots staying busy.

**2026-09-06 — the sandbox disk filled mid-gate, the PC suite launcher replayed itself three times, and the tee verifier returned NOT-READY with 20 findings; the owner asked why suites take 16 minutes and why the PC Hermes lane fell out of use (routing drift corrected).** (1) Disk: three Opus verifiers each materialised full-tree copies (`cp -a` with `.git`: 680 MB; `git archive`: 120-180 MB) plus mutant trees while the coordinator's tree-wide suite ran; the session allowance hit 0 MB at ~09:15 UTC, the sandbox suite run 2 died on `ENOSPC` and the write of a new script was lost. Freed ~4 GB of finished rounds' copies; the two live verifiers were told that every run in the window is VOID (an ENOSPC red is not a kill) and to keep at most one mutant tree. Rules baked (orchestration skill): scratch copies come from `git archive HEAD` never `cp -a` of `.git`; one materialised mutant tree per verifier; the coordinator reads `df` before and during a multi-verifier round. (2) Speed: the sandbox has 4 cores and ran at load 10-12 under the verifiers (CI's 2-vCPU runner: `tests/` in 452 s; the sandbox: 967 s); the heavy parts are the checker suite (~660 s, BIP-340 in ~100 full-pipeline tests), the backend suite (~195 s, a live server per test) and the tee's real stall windows. Compute-placement ruling applied: NEW `scripts/pc_suite.sh` ships the working tree to the PC as one sha-verified binary patch, applies it in a detached worktree at the pushed head and runs pytest with 8 xdist workers (12 cores, load 0.7). Two defects found on its first use, both bridge classics: (a) `a && b && setsid x … &` backgrounds the WHOLE and-list in a subshell that keeps the bridge's stdout pipe open, so the launch call blocked until pytest ended, `pc.sh` retried on the timeout and THREE pytest sessions wrote one log — fixed with a PC-side wrapper script, `;` separation and an in-command state guard (`if [ -s pid ]`), and the replayed sessions were killed by their unique run-dir cwd, never by name; (b) the PC's `/tmp` is a 63 GB tmpfs and one 8-worker run writes ~5 GB of pytest temp files — the three replayed runs filled it and the clean run crashed at startup (`OSError 28` in pytest's capture setup, pytest rc 0 with `no tests ran`) — fixed with `--basetemp` under the disk-backed run dir and a wait that treats a summary without a passed count as RED. PC gate (`scripts/pc_suite.sh launch -n 8 -- tests/ proofs/`, project venv, 12 cores): `4 failed, 1023 passed, 64 skipped in 1345.61s (0:22:25)` — of which ~20 min was the `test_rewrite_is_atomic` pipe deadlock (a fixture agent stuck in `anon_pipe_write`, unblocked by hand; the run's compute time was ~5 min). The four reds are VENUE findings, none in this checkpoint's lane code: `test_agent_burst_within_pipe_buffer_drained` (pipe capacity is not a constant — B5c F-PC-2), `test_probe_identity_fields_all_pinned` + `test_probe_interpreter_fields_pinned` (the fixture agent runs under the shebang interpreter `/usr/bin/python3.13`, the test expects `sys.executable`'s realpath — N5c verify item), and `test_teardown_scan_names_an_owned_survivor_whatever_its_command` (asserts a substring of the survivor's command, which the producer truncates; the venv path is long on the PC — coordinator item on the producer test). Earlier PC runs were void: three replayed launches into one log, then an ENOSPC crash of the PC's tmpfs, then 39 reds under `/usr/bin/python3` because `scripts/validate-ledger` fails closed without `rfc3339-validator` (the project venv carries it; `pytest-xdist` + `PyYAML` added to `pc-setup.sh`). (3) Routing: `scripts/pc_lane.sh … hermes code-implementer` (proven 2026-09-03/04) was not used for the S0-01 repair rounds of 09-05/06 — the bridge was down for part of 09-06 and the rounds' inputs (verifier mutant runners, probes, the real corpus) lived in the sandbox scratchpad — and the coordinator did not switch back when the bridge returned: a drift from the owner's 2026-09-03 ruling (BUILD lanes → Hermes on the PC; the sandbox builder is the fallback only while the bridge is down), costing ~200k coordinator-priced tokens per lane. Corrected from the next build lane on: B5c (tee repair) is dispatched through `pc_lane.sh` with its brief and the verifier's probe scripts committed under `tasks/briefs/s0-01-b5c-*` (the PC reads the repo, not the scratchpad; probes parameterised by `PROBE_WORK`). (4) VERIFY-B5b (tee): NOT-READY — R6-B5b-F1 the audit-P2 drain fix is guarded by nothing (mutant A1 reverts it with 68/68 green and reproduces the audit signature); F2 the round-5 test carrying that shape was deleted undisclosed; F3 an unwritable c2a directional file hangs the tee forever (the `finally` re-raises before closing stdin); F4 a race-dependent test fails the contended gate 2/2 (AF-AP-46 sibling); F5 the A21d snapshot is taken outside the timeline lock — 15/20 SIGKILLed legs violate A21d; F6 the SIGTERM status satisfies no A21d arm; F7 `write_errors` unbounded on six arms; F8-F19 as listed in `tasks/briefs/s0-01-b5c-support/verify-B5b.md`. Rulings in the B5c brief (TERM = non-final + `terminated: SIGTERM` + exit 70; the handler raises instead of locking; snapshot under the timeline lock; timeline written before the directional file; latched write_errors with counts; unwritable final status → exit 70; status right after identity; `PINNED_TEE_STATUS_KEYS` in pins.py consumed by the test and later the checker). (5) VERIFY-D5c (backend): NOT-READY — the allow-list framing gate itself HELD (36/36 replayed vectors closed, 192/192 domain cells match an oracle the verifier re-derived from the contract, http.server's own 501 path proven safe with a forged tail); the blockers are in the CREDENTIAL SCREEN: R7-D5c-F1 a whitespace-split token inside a VALID JSON body is recorded verbatim (the raw body reaches the record boundary on one of three call sites; `json.dumps` re-escapes the separator so the whitespace normalization cannot rejoin it), F2 the Expect arm tests truthiness (`Expect:` empty is served WITH its forged tail), F3/F4/F5 the contract's named controls (`Authorization-X` exemption, the fourth normalization, `CL == MAX`) are absent so mutants M48/M41/M20 survive, F6 obs-folded headers hide a Content-Length/Transfer-Encoding from the gate (RFC 9112 §5.2), F7 double percent-encoding defeats the single `unquote`; plus hygiene F8-F17 (18 `>` record counts, 13 substring status checks, a test that depends on another's records, a false docstring on the load-bearing raw-body claim, 8 MiB of padding, 125 s of dead socket timeouts). The verifier's red tests are COMMITTED (`tests/red/test_s0_01_backend_credential_screen.py`: F1/F2/F6/F7 strict-xfail, F3/F4/F5 guards) and lane D5d makes them green (`tasks/briefs/s0-01-d5d-frame-gate-credential-screen.md`). (6) VERIFY-CK7 (checker A5b+A5c): NOT-READY with 47 findings — the headline is one combined mutant with 24 guards disabled that leaves the suite at `3 failed, 238 passed`: 21 of 24 guards, including the audit's own P1 repairs (two-users ingress concurrency + observed serialization, response cardinality, frame classes, envelope validity) shipped with ZERO regression tests (R7-CK-F1-F9); the A20 v2.3 shutdown/teardown rules 4-5 are unenforced (a live pinned-tee row after shutdown or teardown is ACCEPTED, an empty declared owned set makes the shutdown proof vacuous, the header's `buzz_acp_pid` and `pinned_present` have no consumer — F10-F17); a FIFO in the evidence tree hangs the checker with no timeout (F19); blank lines are filtered instead of failed (F18, the A5b 'contract-permitted' classification was circular); leg dirs have no entry allowlist and the secret screen is presence-gated (F20-F21); records/receipts/startup lines under-constrained (F22-F32); the synthetic scan header differs from the producer's (`pinned_present=0` vs `3` for the same body, F33) and `_TEE_STATUS_KEYS` is an unpinned local copy (F34); ~30 substring reason assertions (F36-F37); the suite writes 4.7 GB of temp per run (F43 — the disk-full mechanism). Lane A5d (`tasks/briefs/s0-01-a5d-checker-guards-tested.md`) with the rulings: red-first for every disabled guard, empty bodies + non-empty bound owned sets on every leg, non-regular entries rejected before any read + a CLI wall-clock cap, entry allowlists, exact reasons, per-check assertion counter in `_run_check`, a hardlink-overlay bundle fixture. Pattern across all three verdicts (AF-AP-36 twin, AF-AP-30): lanes implement the contract and test the specimens they thought of; the guard arms nobody attacked stay hollow — the combined-mutant sweep the verifier ran by hand is the cheapest instrument that exposes this and belongs in every lane's exit gate. Coordinator items from the PC gate: `tests/test_s0_01_pc_post_scan.py::test_teardown_scan_names_an_owned_survivor_whatever_its_command` failed on the PC because the PRODUCER clips: procps `ps -eo args` is cut at 80 columns off a tty (the sandbox's ps does not clip, so no sandbox run could see it) — a long interpreter path pushed the survivor's command and would push a pinned path out of the evidence; fixed with `ps -eww` plus the rule that an OWNED row is never dropped by the scan's helper-row filter (so `owned_present`, counted over the full table, equals the owned body rows by construction — the producer/consumer note in VERIFY-CK7), both with producer tests; and `sync-skills.sh --record` silently re-baselines a hand-ported twin that was NOT patched (the orchestration bullet reached `.agents/skills` only because the coordinator diffed the twins by hand) — patch both twins, then record.

**2026-09-06 — round 7: the framing gate rebuilt as an ALLOW-LIST over the input domain (lane D5c), the probe/classifier survivors killed against the verifier's set (lane N5c), and every lane graded by an independent verifier on a scratch copy before the checkpoint (AF-AP-30 recurrence 10 closed at the class; AF-AP-36 twin rule baked into contract-gate).** D5c: `_framing_gate` is the FIRST statement of do_GET/do_POST — header defects → 400, any Transfer-Encoding → 411, duplicate or non-digit Content-Length → 400, GET body → 400 after a bounded read, POST above MAX → 400, Expect → 417, every rejection `Connection: close`; `_carries_secret` screens the path, header names, header values and both body forms under four normalizations (literal, unquoted, whitespace-stripped, both) at the one record boundary; table-driven tests over 192 domain cells each followed by a forged tail (one response, zero records on every rejection). Lane discrepancies ruled in the main loop: a leading-OWS `Content-Length: 5` is stripped by the parser before the gate (RFC 9110 field-value OWS — the gate sees `5`; served on POST, 400 on GET), the 501 arm is dead inside do_GET/do_POST because http.server answers unknown methods first (tested through that path), duplicate non-framing headers collapse in the record (documented, F23). N5c: the round-6 verifier's 14 substantive survivors killed — 55/55 non-equivalent mutants of its 58-mutant set now red (41 pre-existing + 14 new), the three equivalents named with reasons (CI-10 unreachable behind the response-cardinality gate; SR-03 no multi-line reason in the corpus; AP-32 `probe_path` is venue-specific by design); identity fields `agent_child_pid` / `agent_realpath` / `agent_entrypoint_sha256` pinned exactly by a fixture agent that reports its own pid; interpreter sampling is fail-loud (`probe_error` + exit 1) behind a `proc.poll() is None` guard — the N5c verifier grades that guard (a child that exits before sampling must not leave null identity fields accepted downstream); `227 passed in 38.84s` / `227 passed in 37.47s`, pyflakes clean, not_done none. Verifiers (Opus 5, scratch copies): checker (A5b+A5c) verify IN FLIGHT on a git-archive copy of the committed tree (scratchpad/vck7); D5c verify IN FLIGHT on the committed tree plus the two lane files at their pinned sha256 (scratchpad/vd7); B5b (tee) verify IN FLIGHT (scratchpad/vb6; it grades the pre-`712fe01` tee test on its copy); the N5c verify is dispatched on this checkpoint's head. Verdicts and any repair land at checkpoint 8, never in this commit — nothing here is graded by its own builder. Coordinator items landed: the negative-contract builder pinned to ONE live run of the real probe (R6-N5b-F15, AF-AP-42 rule); contract-gate rule — a lane's own mutation report is a claim, the verifier's mutant set is the acceptance bar. NEXT: the four verdicts → repair lanes for every NOT-READY → PC re-capture with the final tools (the negative first: N5c changed the probe's sha again) → golden pin → mutation suite on the REAL bundle → canonical runner on pc-bridge → ledger → owner review.
