- **2026-09-14 23:0xZ — the xhigh arm LOOPED on a brief PREMISE the coordinator never measured (AF-AP-73 at the brief level; the unbounded investigation = AF-AP-76's sibling, the row widened).** Arm B (`pc-n5k-xhigh-v2.md--c6c384a`, the amended N5k brief at `xhigh`) spent its first 60 minutes on item 2 — 45 tool calls, 130k output tokens, no file written — because the brief asserts "VERIFY-N5j's rig measured `LEAKED_FDS_COUNT = 1`: with the flag dropped the agent child inherits the open framedir fd" and demands the SAME census agent report ONE through the real emitter. Measured by the coordinator on the PC (23:0xZ): CPython's `os.open` sets the close-on-exec bit whether or not `O_CLOEXEC` is passed (PEP 446), and the probe's `Popen` keeps `close_fds=True`; a leak needs BOTH `os.set_inheritable(fd, True)` AND `close_fds=False` — the real-emitter negative control is unsatisfiable as written. The verifier's rig claim was copied into the brief unmeasured (the coordinator's own AF-AP-73). Arm A (medium) had flagged the identical non-reproduction in DISCREPANCIES and built an isolated F_SETFD-controlled census (its report, item 2); arm B kept re-deriving the mechanism — a wrong one (`prctl(PR_SET_FDCLOEXEC)` "set by CPython at startup"; the lane read `errno=EPERM` as seccomp) — and Hermes's read-dedup guard fired on the brief (`BLOCKED: … 3 times`). Fixes: AMENDMENT 2 at the top of the N5k brief (the measured truth, the replacement negative control = the isolated census, the structural pin, one DISCREPANCIES line; no mechanism archaeology); `pc-lane.sh`'s FOURTH standing rule — PREMISE CONFLICTS ARE BOUNDED (three experiments, then a DISCREPANCIES line and build-or-stop; never interpreter/library/kernel archaeology; a BLOCKED re-read is a loop signal) with its test_pc_lane.sh assertion; the rule in skill `orchestration`; AF-AP-76 widened to premises. A/B data point 1 (D-028): on an unmeasured premise, `medium` flagged and built (first write at 14 min); `xhigh` investigated for 60+ minutes and wrote nothing — the effort setting amplifies an unbounded investigation.

- **2026-09-14 21:4xZ — a lane LOOPED 47 minutes on a mechanical bar its brief left unbounded (AF-AP-76); the fix went into the tool, the dispatcher and the skill, not into the lane.** N5k (arm A of the effort A/B, the first local-model build lane) finished the build at 4 h 53 m of lane time (`113 passed` ×3, 12 mutants none surviving) and then spent 20 consecutive turns on `report_lint`: every reference it moved was already right — the lint matched tokens case-sensitively (a prose `FIFO`/`TOCTOU` against the code's `fifo`/`toctou`), had no syntax for a line that existed only at the PIN (the file had moved on in the lane's own tree) and printed no fix; the brief's item 8 demanded `MISS 0 AND UNRESOLVED 0` with no procedure, no round cap and no escape hatch, and the model re-derived the lint's rule from its source each turn (no compaction in the window — a loop the BRIEF built, not the model). The coordinator stopped the lane, hand-stamped its refs to land it and dispatched arm B; the owner interrupted: "don't apply the same fix — figure out why it looped and make the briefs better". Root cause and the fixes: (1) `scripts/report_lint.py` — case-insensitive matching, `alias@<sha>:NN` per-ref revision pins, a `fix:` hint on every MISS row (`6 passed` ×2; the two new tests red on the old code); (2) `harness-ports/bin/pc-lane.sh` injects a third STANDING LANE RULE into every prompt — the hints applied for at most THREE rounds, then paste and finish; a brief's `MISS 0` read as a target, never a stop condition (test_pc_lane.sh asserts it; an old brief's bar is read through it); (3) the ten queued briefs amended at their top (N5k, A5l, D5n, B5k, K1, O3; VERIFY-B3/G3/P5c/M3) and VENUE-MAP's report line; (4) the rule baked into skill `orchestration` and CLAUDE.md's pack line. Arm B relaunched as `pc-n5k-xhigh-v2.md` (a NEW lane id: the PC-side replay guard keys on the brief filename + PIN, and the brief changed). Arm A's 47 minutes are excluded from the A/B's wall-clock comparison (a brief defect, not an effort effect). Cost: 47 minutes of lane time plus one harvest by hand; no code lost.

- **2026-09-14 18:1xZ — a lane's self-report about its own context is not evidence (the local model at medium misreported its rules).** Asked in a one-shot at the N5k tree which project context file it had loaded and which branch it may push to, Qwen3.8 answered "AGENTS.md" and "I don't have a stated only-branch rule" — both false: Hermes's prompt builder, reproduced offline at that tree, injects `.hermes.md` whole (24,076 chars, clean scan, the branch rule inside), and a real lane request dump from 2026-09-08 shows the same section on the wire. Rule confirmed (the Reflection Firewall / graded-never-trusted): what a lane SAYS about its instructions, skills or context proves nothing — read the request dump or run the prompt builder. Filed as a quality data point for the medium-vs-xhigh A/B (D-028); no registry row (the class is AF-AP-37's "typed, not pasted", one layer up).

- **2026-09-14 16:3xZ — the PC's GitHub credential refused a tag push AGAIN (HTTP 403 `Permission to … denied to Pxls21`, the 2026-09-08 class); the documented bundle path used for the second time.** The owner's new `accepted/S0-11` tag (on a9c0c55, Good signature) came over the bridge as a 501-byte `git bundle … ^origin/<branch>` (the tag object only), was fetched here after `git tag -d` of the stale local tag, and its object written to `docs/governance/tags/accepted-S0-11.tag`; the sandbox proxy refused the tag push as documented ("the remote end hung up"). Both venues' `check-proof-status` rc 0. The PC credential remains the owner's item; the README's procedure held without edits. One wrinkle for the README: its command block assumes no prior tag — a RE-sign needs `git tag -d accepted/<id>` first and a `--force` push (the owner hit "already exists" territory today; the commands given in chat carried both).

- **2026-09-14 15:5xZ — the first local-route lane overflowed its slot: a build lane's context is bigger than a quarter of the card.** N5k ran eight minutes on `agentfactory-build-local` and went quiet: the server log read `request (96944 tokens) exceeds the available context size (65536 tokens)` four times (97k → 104k tokens) — `-np 4` splits the 262k context into four 65k slots, and Hermes compacts only at 75 % of the context length OmniRoute reports for the combo (200,000; `context_compressor._SMALL_CTX_THRESHOLD_PERCENT`), so it never compacts below ~150k on its own. Every later request fell through the combo to the cloud members (the fallback doing its job at cloud cost; no work product yet). Diagnosis by `ps --ppid` (the harness alive, idle MCP children), the server log's `E srv send_error` lines, GPU 0 %. Fix: `QWEN_SLOTS` default 1 (one 262k slot ≥ the 200k Hermes believes); `install` now restarts the unit when its text changed (`enable --now` is a no-op on a running unit — the changed argv never took effect the first time) and refuses under a live lane pidfile (rc 7); the lane stopped by pid (loop, then harness) and relaunched from scratch on the restarted server (`new slot, n_ctx = 262144`). Rule: a model slot must hold at least the context length the CONSUMER is told (Hermes reads OmniRoute's number, not llama-server's); parallel lanes on the local model need a Hermes-side context cap first (`custom_providers[].context_length` or OmniRoute's per-model catalog value — the follow-up), never a smaller slot.

- **2026-09-14 15:4xZ — the local build-lane model BUILT AND LIVE with no owner step (task #59; owner: "if it can be done just build it").** Three defects found and fixed by the gates before or at first contact, none reaching a lane: (1) the unit's ExecStart quoted with bash `%q` — systemd is not a shell, `\{` would have reached llama-server verbatim (AF-AP-75; caught by the suite's exact-form assertion before the first install); (2) the first live probe returned `finish=length, predicted_n=24, content=''` — Qwen3.8 spends the budget on `reasoning_content` first, so a probe that printed the token count over an empty answer was tactic 7's hollow green (the probe now gates on the content, budget 256); (3) OmniRoute served an identical repeat of the probe in 0.0 s from its response cache — a cached pong proves nothing about the route (the probe carries a nonce). Also learned: the explicit `POST /api/providers/<id>/sync-models` answers 401 to the CLI's machine token on 3.8.50 while the connection's own creation-time import already lists the model (the call is made only while the model is missing, its status information never a gate); `systemd-analyze --user verify` needs `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (the bridge shell has none — the script exports `/run/user/$(id -u)`); the sandbox dispatcher run from a private copy needs `PC_LANE_ORIG` AND the bridge env exported (`set -a; . .pc-bridge.env`) — three dead launches before the fourth. Live: the unit up in 8 s, Hermes end-to-end on the combo with a real tool call in 28 s, N5k dispatched as the first build lane on the local route.

- **2026-09-14 12:3xZ — the hollow lint: a report with no machine-checkable reference lints clean (AF-AP-74).** Writing VERIFY-B3, the coordinator ran `report_lint` on B3's report at its landing and read `0 refs — OK 0, NEAR 0, MISS 0 …` rc 0: the lane had cited every claim in prose (`line ~120`, `line 651-vicinity`) and the gate, which counts MISSes over the report's own citations, had nothing to miss — the omission VERIFY-B2 item 0 had graded on round 2, repeated on round 3 and passing the checkpoint's lint by construction. Fixed the same turn: `--min-refs N` (rc 1 + a FLOOR line under N OK refs; the old contract unchanged without the flag), `4 passed` ×2, the floor-removed mutant red on a scratch copy; the rule baked into `orchestration` (c2) and CLAUDE.md's pack line — a checkpoint gates on a floor, never on the MISS count alone. Sibling lesson the same hour: the coordinator's own landing amendment (`_PY_DIRFD`, +9 lines) shifted P5c's test refs +8/+9 and the lane's report went from 15/15 to `OK 15, MISS 1` at the landing — lint a lane's report at the LANDING rev, not only at the lane's PIN (the joint-landing class VERIFY-P5b F1 named; stamped atop the P5c report). And a tooling quirk that cost one probe run: `pkill -f "[r]un_packs.sh"` killed the coordinator's own shell (rc 144) because the same command line named the script in a later `sed`/`nohup` argument — the bracket trick protects only the pattern; kill by pid.



**2026-09-16 — the build-verify loop looped: no blocking predicate + a resettable round cap reopened completed work (D-031, the round-level generalization of AF-AP-76).** A Codex audit of the verifier role found the cause of the endless build→verify rounds: "your job is to make the change fail" + "report EVERYTHING, no severity floor" + a binary MERGE-READY/NOT-READY verdict + a round cap that could reset under a renamed or restarted wave meant marginal, speculative, out-of-scope and proof-of-proof findings automatically reopened finished increments. Fix (D-031; no new AF-AP number — AF-AP-76's lesson lifted from the mechanical-bar/premise level to the round level): an exhaustive FINDING INVENTORY (no severity filter on discovery, classes `BLOCKER`/`FOLLOW-UP`/`INFO`/`UNVERIFIED`) split from a GATE RECOMMENDATION (`MERGE-READY` / `MERGE-READY-WITH-FOLLOWUPS` / `NOT-READY` / `CONTRACT-INVALID`; the coordinator owns the final gate); a strict five-condition blocking predicate (contract-mapped · canonically reproduced through the real production path at the PIN · materially effective · a concrete discriminator · in-boundary) so a red test is NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT; a repair budget of ONE focused repair (`build → independent verify → one focused repair → reverify`) keyed by `component/proof ID + frozen contract revision + production-code digest` that does NOT reset on a renamed wave, a new verifier, or a report/test/mutation/policy-only commit; a `CONTRACT-DEFECT` escape hatch that returns to the coordinator for an explicit amendment; and workload routing (pure logic → normal review; stateful boundary → one focused independent verification; proof/minting → the full apparatus). Wired canonical-first into `.claude/agents/adversarial-verifier.md` + `harness-ports/roles/adversarial-verifier.md` (→ `.codex/agents/*` via `build-roles.py`), skills `contract-gate`/`build-loop`/`orchestration`/`anti-hollow-green`, `harness-ports/briefs/verify-contract.md`, and the CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/.hermes.md mirrors; no proof, ledger, production component, active lane, remote workflow, or live service was touched. Rule: preserve exhaustive REPORTING, remove exhaustive BLOCKING — a verifier with no blocking predicate and no bounded budget is a loop the process built.

Design signature (missing paired control), not an AP_SCREEN regex; project sweep found no other isolation assertions. **Cycle-4 reopen:** the active network discriminator FAILED OPEN — `nsenter` returning `None` (never ran, e.g. non-root) was accepted as a pass, so the loopback discriminator never ran in green CI. Re-swept: it is now a REQUIRED PAIRED control (wrapped `net_reachable is False`, un-wrapped `is True`) and the preflight requires `nsenter` to actually run, deferring (exit 2) where it cannot rather than fail-open. Rule sharpened: an active discriminator that returns "could not run" is a DEFER, never a pass. `test_wrapped_child_has_fresh_cwd_and_refuses_listener`, `test_parent_observes_unwrapped_child_as_breached` |
| AF-AP-23 | OPEN-ENDED filter passed off as an allow-list — a credential stripper keyed on a NAME blacklist, a sweep gated on a FILE-EXTENSION allowlist, OR an env allow-list keyed on a NAME PREFIX (`RUBRIC_*`): each silently passes an out-of-set member. A prefix is not a closed set. | an env "clean" builder that `continue`s on named keys / a suffix set; a sweep `if path.suffix not in (…)`; `k.startswith("RUBRIC_")` (or any prefix) as the env allow rule | S0-11 round 1: `_clean_env` blacklist + `.py`/`.sh`-only sweep. S0-11 round 2: the "fix" allow-listed every `RUBRIC_*` by prefix → `RUBRIC_PRODUCTION_API_KEY` passed (owner review 2026-09-04). | SWEPT(2026-09-04 r2) — env is now a CLOSED EXACT set (`ENV_ALLOWLIST ∪ {RUBRIC_TASK_ID,RUBRIC_CWD,RUBRIC_PROBE_PORT}`, no prefix); `test_allowlist_is_closed_exact_set`. Design signature. RECURRED once as a prefix — the fix for a blacklist must be an EXACT allow-list, never a prefix. |
| AF-AP-24 | proxy capability preflight — a skip/availability guard tests a PROXY signal (a uid change) instead of the EXACT predicate the real check consumes, so it green-lights a host the real check then hard-fails | a `skipif`/availability probe that checks one capability while the checked code reads several (`/proc/self/ns/net`, a listener, a uid); preflight and consumer diverge | S0-11 round-2 pytest `_isolation_available` checked only uid; on a host with `/proc/self/ns/net` unreadable it ran and the positive check failed (owner: 2 failed) | SWEPT(2026-09-04) — the preflight IS the checker's own `--selftest` (parent+child netns readable + `unshare --user --net` runs); positive returns exit 2 (capability-unavailable) so pytest skips, never fails. Rule = preflight the exact consumer predicate. **Round-3 extension:** EVERY namespace-reading leg is gated, not a selected subset (round-2 left the four-axis negative test ungated → the owner's host failed it); `test_positive_defers_on_incapable_host` proves the incapable path returns exit 2. Verify every leg under BOTH a capable and an incapable environment. **Cycle-4 reopen:** "every leg gated" was still only the pytest DECORATOR — the checker's own `--rubric-neg` CLI mode never ran the preflight, so the canonical runner did not defer consistently on an incapable venue. Re-swept: every namespace-reading CLI leg (`positive`, `--rubric-neg`) runs `_capability_status` and returns exit 2; `test_every_namespace_leg_defers_on_incapable`. Gate the CONSUMER (the CLI leg), not just its test. **Cycle-5 reopen (recurrence 3):** the cycle-4 gating lived in the CHECKER; the CANONICAL runner (`scripts/proof-runner`) itself did NOT defer — it removed result.json FIRST then failed `leg-exit-mismatch … got 2`, destroying the capable-venue artifact, and the "canonical-invocation" test actually invoked the checker legs directly (skipped on incapable CI, so never exercised the runner). Re-swept: the runner treats exit 2 as DEFER and PRESERVES the artifact; `test_canonical_runner_defers_and_preserves_on_incapable_venue` + `test_canonical_runner_runs_on_capable_venue` drive the REAL runner in BOTH venue states. Rule: execute the actual consumer, in both venue states — a proxy test that bypasses the consumer proves nothing about it. Design signature. **Recurrence 2026-09-23 (S0-05, measured):** the unit runner's preflight (`run_s0_05_units.sh:354-359`) passed any HTTP answer while the checker needs a 2xx on the collector's request; the real OmniRoute answers that request 401 and the real relay 404, so the preflight would launch units the checker then fails — E3-b R2 makes the preflight grade the checker's predicate on the same request (AF-AP-139 is the stand-in half) |
| AF-AP-25 | regex-list gate where a PARSER is required — a security check scans structured input (Python, YAML, JSON) with regexes, so real calls/keys in a form the regex does not spell out slip through, and the checker must self-exclude to avoid matching its own pattern strings | a `re`-only sweep over `.py`/`.yaml` for security ops; a checker that excludes itself from its own sweep | S0-11 round-2 sweep missed `os.chmod(…,0o777)`, `subprocess.run(["chmod","-R","0777"])`, `hostNetwork: True`; self-exclusion hid an op in the checker file | SWEPT(2026-09-04) — Python via AST (call-based, ignores string literals → no self-exclusion), YAML via `safe_load` walk, regex only for shell/text. **Round-3 extension:** even AST is specimen-matching (missed `import subprocess as sp`, `from subprocess import run`, `cmd=[…]; run(cmd)`, `mode=0o777`, `0o700\|0o077`, `network_mode: ${VAR:-host}`, and prose "isolation unnecessary / chmod 777 mandatory"). Two-part fix: (a) the DESIGN gate is now a machine-readable ```yaml `policy:` block the checker parses — prose cannot satisfy or invert it; (b) the sweep is reframed as a BEST-EFFORT LINT (alias resolution + constant folding added) whose limits are stated — the real boundary is the runtime isolation + gVisor, not a complete static scan. `test_sweep_catches_mutant` (12 equivalents), `test_runner_design_{ignores_inverted_prose,rejects_permissive_policy,rejects_missing_policy}`. Siblings OPEN: S0-09/S0-10 checkers still use regex matching (owner "3 checkers"). Design signature. **2026-09-23 (VERIFY-REPIN-a F11; bug-echo `docs/research/bug-echo/2026-09-23-bug-echo-fail-open-line-parser.md`): the class on a GATE path, with a fail-open twist** — `scripts/vendored_manifest.py` `parse_lock` and `parse_sbom` read the lock and the SBOM with `re.fullmatch` and SKIP a line they do not recognise, so one trailing space on a lock component line, or a YAML comment on an SBOM `- name:` line, drops a pin from `validate_pin_agreement` (pre-commit and pre-push) with no error; both REPRODUCED by the coordinator (24 → 23 lock pins; 22 → 21 SBOM entries with one pin re-attached to the wrong name). OPEN → task #170 (refuse every unrecognised line by number, or `yaml.safe_load` with a duplicate-key refusal). The sweep's other 18 sites fail closed, are lookups, or are JSONL (1 WATCH, 2 REVIEW in the report). The edit-snapshot screen line LANDED in K150 (task #150, 656ddf6; VERIFY-K150 MERGE-READY-WITH-FOLLOWUPS, 2026-09-24). |
| AF-AP-26 | permissive-default / relative-only security predicate — a validator reads evidence with a safe default (`report.get(k, [])`) so MISSING evidence passes, or asserts only a relative bound (`uid != parent`) without the absolute floor (`uid != 0`) | `.get(field)`/`.get(field, <benign>)` on a required field in a gate; an identity/limit check that omits the absolute boundary | S0-11 round-2 `_violations` accepted `uid==0` for a non-root parent and defaulted missing `uid`/`env_keys` to a pass | SWEPT(2026-09-04) — mandatory-field check rejects a malformed report; uid axis asserts `!= parent AND != 0`. **Round-3 extension:** `isinstance(True, int)` and `isinstance(-1, int)` are BOTH true, so `uid: true`/`uid: -1` slipped the "typed" check — a numeric evidence field needs exact type + range (`type(x) is int and x >= 0`), never bare `isinstance(_, int)`. Here it is dissolved entirely: the uid is parsed from `/proc/<pid>/status` (kernel-supplied unsigned int), not a child-authored JSON value. `test_report_validation_root_and_missing`. Design signature |
| AF-AP-27 | a FROZEN contract (seed/registry) reshaped in an implementation without an explicit owner decision — the negative control the seed pinned is deleted or its reason changed under cover of an unrelated fix | a spec/registry edit that drops or rewrites a seed-declared `negative_control`/`expected_failure_reason`/frozen mapping; the change appears only in the diff, never surfaced | S0-11 round-1 deleted the frozen `rubric-isolation-violation: credential env absent by construction` control; registry repair reshaped mappings (owner: "2 examples") | SWEPT(2026-09-04) — frozen credential control restored as its own leg, four-axis added as an ADDITIONAL leg; the spec test asserts the frozen reason is present. Rule = a frozen contract changes only by an explicit owner decision, never as a side effect. Process signature |
| AF-AP-28 | a security assertion trusts the SUBJECT's self-report — the isolated process reports its own uid/netns/env and the checker believes it; a fake/pass-through wrapper fabricates a clean report. Sibling: an isolation/privilege binary resolved via `PATH` can be replaced. | a checker that reads the child's stdout/JSON for its own isolation state; `unshare`/`nsenter`/`setpriv` invoked by bare name (PATH-resolved) rather than absolute path | S0-11 round-2 read a child-authored JSON report of uid/netns/env; `unshare --user --net` even makes the child self-report uid 65534 while its host uid stays 0 | SWEPT(2026-09-04) — the child only signals ready + blocks; the PARENT reads kernel truth from `/proc/<pid>/status|ns/net|environ` and `nsenter`s the child netns to test the listener; wrappers are absolute-path; a real privilege drop (`setpriv --reuid`) is used. `test_parent_observes_unwrapped_child_as_breached`, `test_positive_fails_without_real_isolation`. Rule = observe the subject from OUTSIDE (kernel/parent), never trust its self-report. Design signature |
| AF-AP-29 | the machine-checkable CONTRACT is weaker than the test — the canonical runner accepts a result a committed test would reject (a spec `failure_reason` prefix vs the full reason; a schema looser than an assertion) | a proof/spec `expect.failure_reason` that is a prefix/substring while the test asserts the complete string; any canonical gate laxer than the strongest test of the same property | S0-11 round-2 spec required only `rubric-isolation-violation:`; the test required all axes, so a one-axis result passed the runner | SWEPT(2026-09-04) — the spec pins the COMPLETE deterministic reason (the venue-stable pair `…: env-not-allowlisted,netns-not-isolated`; the uid axis is venue-dependent so it is not in the pinned reason, and is exercised by the positive non-vacuity gate on a root venue); `test_spec_valid_and_contract_matches_test`. Rule = the canonical contract is at least as strong as the strongest test of the same claim. Process signature |
| AF-AP-30 | specimen-only remediation — a review's named cases are fixed and the whole CLASS declared closed, so the same defect re-appears one equivalent out (prefix→exact, blacklist→prefix, regex→AST, child-report→parent-observe) | a "fixed" claim after a review whose tests cover only the reported instances; no equivalence-class mutants (alias/computed/defaulted forms, boundary values, the observe-vs-report axis) | S0-11 reopened FIVE times (rounds 1→2→3→cycle-4→cycle-5); each fix passed its own cases and the next review broke the adjacent equivalent (blacklist→prefix→exact; regex→AST→policy-block; child-report→parent-observe; net-tautology→active-probe-fail-open→required-paired-control; pytest-gate→CLI-gate→canonical-runner-defer; claimed-cwd→observed-cwd→writable/collected-cwd; artifact-trusted→artifact-bound-to-source→bound-to-the-COMPLETE-closure; acceptance word-ban→hidden-marker→visible-single-source-owner-anchored) | OPEN (meta-class; the discipline, not a single site) — recurrence count 8 (cycle-8: the cycle-7 status guard fixed the hidden-marker and self-accept SPECIMENS but left the slug-keyed-row equivalent — a canonical-slug `DONE` row passed because only BARE-id rows were rejected; the class "authoritative review state across surfaces" needed the one visible `PROOF-STATUS` marker BOUND to the one canonical task row via an EXACT proof→slug map. This is where the meta-class finally resolved: the owner accepted the proof as a process decision and moved the residual anchor work to a separate task, so the loop ends by naming the guard's real scope rather than fixing an eighth equivalent) (cycle-7: the cycle-6 status guard fixed the word-ban SPECIMEN but left three equivalents — a hidden marker diverging from a visible row, an arbitrary self-accept file, and that file breaking the attestation; the class "authoritative review state" needed a single visible source + no coordinator-writable acceptance) (cycle-6: the cycle-5 attestation bound only the proof-local files, so mutating the RUNNER/VALIDATOR left the green standing, and the validator never compared the recorded runs to the attested spec — the class "trust binding" was fixed for the named files and left open for the tooling that runs/checks them). Mitigation: `anti-hollow-green` now mandates EQUIVALENCE-CLASS mutation testing (test the class, not the specimen), binding the COMPLETE trust closure (every file that produces OR checks the verdict), and exercising the CANONICAL CONSUMER under both a capable and an incapable environment. The tell: a review that clears the named cases is the moment to attack the class before claiming closure. Behavioral signature; recurrence 9 (2026-09-05, S0-01: five owner mutations passed the checker after minting — the class is the missing pre-mint gate, AF-AP-36); recurrence 10 (2026-09-06, backend framing gate: D5 → D5b → D5c, three rounds of vector-by-vector closure each leaving the adjacent equivalent open — resolved by the allow-list redesign, AF-AP-47) |
| AF-AP-31 | proof output not bound to its executable inputs — a recorded result artifact carries command/output hashes but not the digest of the CODE/spec/fixtures that produced it, so neutering the checker and keeping the stale green still validates; the gate re-validates the artifact without re-running the proof | a `result.json`/report schema whose fields hash only the run I/O (a positive hash that is merely `sha256("PASS\n")`); a validator that checks schema+digest but recomputes nothing from the source tree; CI that validates the recorded artifact and never re-executes | S0-11 cycle-5: `_iso_launch` replaced with a pass-through → `validate-ledger integrity` still returned `S0-11 PRESENT` exit 0 (owner reproduced) | SWEPT(2026-09-04 cycle-5) — the runner records an `attestation` (sha256 of every input file under the proof dir), the validator re-derives it from the tree and fails on any mismatch, and `result.schema.json` REQUIRES it across all four present artifacts; `test_attestation_binds_artifact_to_source`, `test_attestation_mismatch_flips_present_to_invalid`, `test_result_schema_requires_attestation`. Rule: bind the artifact to its inputs (attest the producing code), and re-run on a capable venue — a validator that never re-derives from source trusts a frozen lie. **Cycle-6 reopen (the binding was INCOMPLETE):** the cycle-5 attestation hashed only `proofs/<id>/**`, so mutating the canonical `scripts/proof-runner` (every leg → `/usr/bin/true`) made the run fail while the STALE green artifact was preserved and `validate-ledger integrity` still returned PRESENT; separately, forging the recorded positive command to `/usr/bin/true` and recomputing the self-digest was accepted because the validator never compared the recorded `runs` to the attested `spec.json`. Three-part re-sweep: (a) `proof_attestation` now covers the COMPLETE TRUST CLOSURE — the runner, this validator, the registry and the schemas, not only the proof dir; (b) the validator binds the recorded runs to the attested spec one-for-one (count, order, leg, command, exit, and each negative leg's `failure_reason`) so a forged command no longer matches; (c) the runner INVALIDATES (removes) the artifact on any real capable-run failure — only an explicit defer (exit 2) preserves it. `test_forged_command_fails_runs_spec_binding`, `test_registry_mutation_breaks_attestation`, `test_tooling_mutation_breaks_attestation`, `test_real_run_failure_invalidates_artifact`, `test_committed_runs_are_bound_to_spec`. Rule sharpened: attest EVERYTHING that produces or checks the verdict, bind the recorded run to the attested spec, and never preserve an artifact across a real failure. Schema/validator signature; recurrence (2026-09-05, S0-01: manifests, process facts, route and identity were authored record fields the checker compared to themselves — bind to raw artifacts derived at run time) |
| AF-AP-32 | status precedes owner acceptance — the coordinator records a proof/task as closed ("re-closed", DONE) while it is still under the owner's active review, so the ledger claims completion the owner has not granted | a build-ledger row or task that marks an owner-reopened item closed with no owner-acceptance record; a chat handoff that says "review-pending" contradicting the tracked status | S0-11: the tasklist said self-closure while the handoff said review-pending (owner's 5th review) | SWEPT(2026-09-04 cycle-5) — a proof the owner reopened is REVIEW-PENDING until the owner accepts; the coordinator never self-records closure. Mechanically enforced by a `verify-planning-repo.sh` guard that bans the self-closure vocabulary in `BUILD-TASKLIST.md` (runs in CI's `planning` job). Rule: the owner grants closure; the coordinator reports REVIEW-PENDING and lets the owner accept. Process signature (ledger-vocabulary guard, not an AP_SCREEN regex). **Cycle-6 reopen (a vocabulary check is not a STATE guard):** the cycle-5 guard rejected only the literal `re-?closed`, so a `S0-11 | DONE by coordinator without owner acceptance` row passed. Re-swept: a structured parser (`scripts/check-proof-status.py`) reads the canonical machine-readable marker `<!-- PROOF-STATUS S0-11 REVIEW-PENDING -->` and enforces STATE — exactly one marker per proof (a conflicting duplicate fails), status ∈ {REVIEW-PENDING, ACCEPTED} (DONE/CLOSED/any other word fails), ACCEPTED requires a committed owner-acceptance record `proofs/<id>/OWNER-ACCEPTED`, and a proof under review must carry a marker (deleting it fails). `tests/test_proof_status.py` covers DONE, CLOSED, deletion, duplicate, ACCEPTED-with/without-record. This is the same regex-vs-parser class as AF-AP-25 (a regex vocabulary check is not a semantic state guard); the S0-09/S0-10 regex checkers are the remaining siblings. Process signature. **Cycle-7 reopen (the cycle-6 fix was itself three AF-AP-32 instances):** (a) the marker was a HIDDEN HTML comment, so a VISIBLE `\| S0-11 \| DONE by coordinator \|` task row contradicted it and the guard passed (the human ledger and the machine state diverged); (b) the `proofs/<id>/OWNER-ACCEPTED` record was an ARBITRARY FILE — file existence is not authentication, so the builder self-accepted by committing a file saying "created by implementation agent"; (c) that record lived INSIDE the attested proof dir, so following the acceptance procedure invalidated the attestation (`attestation-mismatch`). Re-swept: the status is now a SINGLE VISIBLE line `PROOF-STATUS: <id> = <status>` (hidden HTML-comment markers are rejected); the coordinator may record ONLY REVIEW-PENDING (there is NO coordinator-writable ACCEPTED — any acceptance file the coordinator can write it can forge); a Markdown task row keyed by a bare proof id is rejected (the contradictory-visible-row attack); and no review metadata lives in the attested proof dir. The deeper rule: **the coordinator must not build an acceptance gate it can satisfy — genuine acceptance is an OWNER action on owner-controlled infrastructure (a merge to `main`, or a protected review) that the coordinator structurally cannot forge; the in-repo guard enforces REVIEW-PENDING and rejects self-acceptance, and the owner-verifiable ACCEPTED anchor is an owner decision, surfaced not fabricated.** `tests/test_proof_status.py` covers the hidden-marker, bare-id-row, coordinator-cannot-ACCEPT, duplicate, and REVIEW-PENDING→both-gates cases. **Cycle-8 resolution (owner accepted S0-11; do not reopen):** the last equivalent was the SLUG-keyed row — the cycle-7 guard rejected only bare-id rows, so `\| s0-18-s0-11-eval-hardening \| … \| DONE \|` passed while the `PROOF-STATUS` line stayed REVIEW-PENDING (owner-reported, reproduced rc=0). Fixed by BINDING the single visible `PROOF-STATUS: <id> = <status>` line to the ONE canonical task row: exactly one row keyed by the proof's canonical slug via an EXACT proof→slug map (never a substring — the S0-10 row's slug `s0-11-s0-10-gbrain-adr` embeds "s0-11" and a substring match misbinds it), and that row's status-cell leading token must equal the marker's value. The owner then ruled acceptance a HUMAN PROCESS DECISION, not machine-enforcement: while the agent pushes under the owner's own GitHub identity no in-repo status is structurally owner-only, so this guard now checks CONSISTENCY (surfaces agree), NOT authenticity, and ACCEPTED is a valid recorded status that the guard does not — and cannot — verify was truly the owner's. The owner-verifiable anchor (a dedicated bot/GitHub-App identity + protected `main` + the owner's native GitHub review on the head SHA) is the separate `acceptance-anchor-af-ap-32` task, owner-blocked on infrastructure. Rules baked: bind a state marker across EVERY visible surface that displays it; use an exact key map, never a substring, when one slug embeds another proof's id; a guard that can only check consistency must SAY so, never be sold as enforcing owner-only acceptance the pusher's identity cannot make owner-only. Process signature |
| AF-AP-33 | health-200 ≠ the right instance — a duplicate/unmanaged process squats the service port and answers health while serving a divergent dataset or config, so port-level liveness reads green during an outage of the real service | a liveness check that hits only `/health` or `/api/health`; a systemd unit reported "active" inside an `EADDRINUSE` restart loop; two processes with the same binary name and different `HOME`/data dirs | OmniRoute 2026-09-05: an orphan on `:20128` served the small `~/.omniroute` DB (catalog ~622 vs 2,626; combos gone; Ollama Cloud "no credentials") while health said 200 | SWEPT(2026-09-05) — `scripts/omniroute_invariants.sh`: exactly one listener, its cgroup = the managed unit, its OWN environ carries the authoritative `DATA_DIR` (+ `REQUIRE_API_KEY=true`), health, and the authenticated catalog carries every required id; edit-snapshot screen flags health-only probes |

| AF-AP-94 | VERIFY-THEN-REPARSE TOCTOU, AND GRADING A SUBPROCESS VERIFIER BY A SUBSTRING — two ways a cryptographic/external verification is defeated while the crypto itself is sound. This is AF-AP-38 (substring where the contract says `==`) recurring on a subprocess verifier. | a `.py`/`.sh` that runs `gpg --verify <p>` or a hash check on a path and LATER re-reads `<p>`; `"GOODSIG"`/`"OK"`/`"VALID" in <verifier>.stdout` instead of a status-line + exit-code check; trusting a verifier's `rc == 0` without its revoked/expired semantics | 2026-09-16/17 GOV2c (VERIFY-GOV2b F1/F2): `review.py` verified the review record then re-read it (117 of 400 UNSIGNED records accepted under a write race) and accepted a `REVKEYSIG` signature carrying a `GOODSIG` notation (revocation disabled). | SWEPT: snapshot-verify-parse the same bytes (F1 → 0/400); a `[GNUPG:] GOODSIG ` line-prefix + `VALIDSIG` fingerprint pin + one-key owner file (F2/F6 → `REVKEYSIG` refused); 6 red-first tests + mutation 4/4. A code-hunk signature the edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN can carry: a verify call + a later re-read of the same path, or a substring test over a verifier's stdout. |

META-LESSON (the rule bit TWICE): the correction itself — "the ACP agent needs an ACTIVE stored credential, not a config-resolvable one" — was ALSO a source-read hypothesis, and it too was FALSIFIED by the live 3-way OmniRoute probe (the real root cause is AF-AP-103: the provider prefix leaked into the wire model name; credentials were never the problem, `resolve_runtime_provider()` resolves the key cleanly). Only the direct HTTP probe against the real aggregator settled it. General rule reinforced doubly: build-loop step 4 and deep-work Phase 0 — a source-read is a HYPOTHESIS until the live production path agrees; probe the actual boundary (here: OmniRoute itself with bare vs prefixed model), do not reason a second theory from more source. A claim-discipline signature, not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN `.py` code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-103 | A HERMES-INTERNAL PROVIDER-SELECTOR PREFIX LEAKED INTO THE WIRE MODEL NAME AND THE AGGREGATOR RETURNED A 401 THAT READS LIKE A LOCAL CREDENTIAL FAILURE — a custom OpenAI-compatible aggregator provider is selected by a `custom:<name>` label that is INTERNAL to the client (Hermes reads it from `model.provider` to pick the provider's base_url + key). Put that label in `model.default` and the client forwards it VERBATIM as the model id; the aggregator (OmniRoute) parses the leading `custom:<name>/` as a provider it has no connection for and returns `401 No active credentials for provider: <name>`. The message names your OWN internal label, so it reads as a client-side credential/resolution failure and sends you chasing credentials for hours — five config/credential shapes and an in-process `resolve_runtime_provider()` (which resolved the key CLEANLY every time) before a direct 3-way HTTP probe isolated it. Rule: the model id on the wire is the aggregator's BARE combo/route id; the provider selector prefix belongs only on `model.provider` and must never appear in `model.default`. A provider-auth 401 whose message names your own internal provider label is THIS class — probe the aggregator directly with the bare vs prefixed model BEFORE touching credentials. | a `custom:<name>/…` (or any client-internal provider-selector prefix) appearing in `model.default` / the model id sent to an aggregator; a provider-auth error from an aggregator naming a provider label that is the CLIENT's config name, not one of the aggregator's own connections; chasing credential resolution when the wire model name carries a selector prefix | 2026-09-18 (S0-03 leg B): `model.default: custom:s0-03-omniroute/agentfactory-build` → OmniRoute 401 "No active credentials for provider: custom:s0-03-omniroute"; the direct probe: `model=agentfactory-build` → 200 (real gpt-5.6-sol-ultra), `model=custom:s0-03-omniroute/agentfactory-build` → the exact 401. Fix: `model.default: agentfactory-build` (bare) + `model.provider: custom:s0-03-omniroute`; leg B then completes a real tool-call round trip (33 frames, `printf <nonce>` → completed, the nonce in the agent's final message). | FIXED + PROVEN LIVE 2026-09-18 — `proofs/S0-03/LIVE-CAPTURE-FINDINGS.md`; the config/tooling realign + mint is the follow-on. A config/wire-shape signature (a provider-selector prefix in the wire model id), not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN `.py` code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-104 | A READ-ONLY SQLITE INSTRUMENT OPENED `immutable=1` MISSES ROWS STILL IN THE `-wal` FILE ON A LIVE WAL DATABASE — `file:…?immutable=1` reads ONLY the main database file and never consults the `-wal`, so on a database the live service runs in WAL mode any row not yet checkpointed out of the WAL is INVISIBLE to the reader; a durably-committed fresh row can stay invisible for minutes (until an auto-checkpoint at ~1000 WAL pages). An evidence collector that binds a just-made request to its recorded row then reads `0 rows` and fails — intermittently, depending on WAL-checkpoint timing and unrelated traffic. Rule: read a LIVE WAL database `mode=ro` (read-only AND WAL-aware), never `immutable=1`; reserve `immutable=1` for a quiescent/copied DB no process is writing. | a `sqlite3.connect(f"file:{db}?immutable=1")` (or CLI `?immutable=1`) against a database another process writes in WAL mode; an evidence/log reader that binds a fresh row and intermittently reports it missing; any "the row is in a normal query but not the instrument's" discrepancy | 2026-09-19 (S0-03 collect_leg.sh, coordinator live measurement): the direct-leg collector opened OmniRoute's storage.sqlite `immutable=1` and found `direct=0` for a just-succeeded /v1/responses request; a side-by-side open proved it — `immutable=1` reported `journal_mode delete` and its newest visible row was minutes stale, while `mode=ro` reported `journal_mode wal` and saw the fresh row at once (the nonce present in its artifact). | FIXED + PROVEN LIVE 2026-09-19 — collect_leg.sh `immutable=1` → `mode=ro`; it now binds `direct=1` (provider=codex, combo=agentfactory-build, status 200, nonce in recorded_input). `proofs/S0-03/LIVE-CAPTURE-FINDINGS.md` Blocker 4. A sqlite-open-mode signature (immutable vs mode=ro on a live WAL DB), not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN `.py` code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-105 | A CAPTURE RUNNER THAT REUSES A FIXED-NAME OUTPUT DIR AND POLLS FOR A READINESS MARKER RACES THE PRIOR RUN'S STALE MARKER — a runner that launches a DETACHED producer into a dir keyed by a fixed name (`<markers>/v2-<leg>`) then polls for the producer's `launch.ready` sees the PRIOR run's stale `launch.ready` (+ `runtime-identity.json`, pidfiles) on its FIRST poll, before the producer's own startup cleanup runs — so the leg binds the prior run's DEAD pid/state. The producer rmtree-ing on start and writing the marker LAST is not enough: the detached launch + immediate poll loses the race to the leftover. Rule: the CALLER removes the reused dir (or at least the readiness/completion markers) BEFORE launching the detached producer; never trust a readiness marker whose freshness the caller has not guaranteed. | a runner that `launch X &` into a name-reused dir then polls `[ -f <dir>/<marker> ]` with no pre-clean; a leg binding a pid/identity from a file the current launch has not provably rewritten; a `readlink /proc/<pid>/exe FileNotFoundError` on a pid far below the current pid range | 2026-09-19 (S0-03 run_s0_03_legs.sh leg B): the second live re-capture reused framedir `.markers/v2-s0-03-hermes` still holding v6's (2026-09-18) `launch.ready` + `runtime-identity.json`; the first poll saw the stale marker, read `agent_child_pid 2941354` (a v6 pid, dead), and `hermes_env_names.py` failed `readlink /proc/2941354/exe`. pc_launch.py DOES rmtree the framedir (:308) and write launch.ready last (:419), but the runner polled before that ran. | FIXED 2026-09-19 — run_s0_03_legs.sh removes `$framedir` (`rm -rf`) immediately before launching pc_launch, so the readiness poll can only see this run's fresh marker; pc_launch's own rmtree is then a no-op. SIBLING found 2026-09-21 by the VB-F12 recapture brief's ancestry pass: S0-01's `proofs/S0-01/tools/pc/run_leg.sh` (167e063, 2026-09-05) had the identical shape (only `v2-<leg>.launch.log` pre-removed, the framedir not) — fixed the same way (`rm -rf $FD` on the launch call ahead of the setsid) + `test_run_leg_removes_the_framedir_before_the_detached_launch` (RED on the 167e063 driver, GREEN on the fix). A capture-runner-readiness signature (name-reused output dir + poll for a marker the caller did not freshen), not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN `.py` code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-106 | A RESIDUAL-SELECTION CHECK THAT PICKS "NEWEST MATCH" FOR A COMMITTED-CONSTANT KEY AGAINST A PERSISTENT PRODUCER SILENTLY REUSES A PRIOR RUN'S ARTIFACT WHEN THE CURRENT ACTION PRODUCED NONE — when a producer's output store persists across runs (a shared backend you do NOT own, so you cannot pre-clean it — the AF-AP-105 sibling for the un-cleanable case) and the selection key is a COMMITTED CONSTANT (a fixture nonce/id → one artifact per run), "the record carrying the key" matches one-per-run and "newest wins" returns a PRIOR run's artifact whenever THIS action wrote none (a failed POST/write → a stale same-key record graded as a pass = false green). Rule: capture a PRE-ACTION baseline (the max seq/id present BEFORE the action) and require EXACTLY ONE match STRICTLY PAST it — zero fails LOUD (the action produced nothing, proven), never "newest"; the baseline arg is fail-closed (required, no default). | a find-record/select-latest over a persistent store keyed on a constant nonce/id with no pre-action floor; a "newest matching row/file wins" residual selection where a no-op action would reuse a prior artifact; an evidence copy that cannot distinguish this run's output from an identical prior run's | 2026-09-19 (S0-04 capture_leg.find_record): the routing fix reused the live persistent :20201 backend (records accumulate, fixture nonce constant), so find-record matched 2 records per nonce (030089 prior + 030097 this run); "newest wins" would have reused 030089 had this POST failed. Fix: --max-seq baseline before the POST + --after-seq (required); exactly-one-past-baseline, zero fails loud. | FIXED 2026-09-19 — capture_leg.py find_record(dir, nonce, after_seq) + max_seq; run_s0_04_legs.sh captures the baseline per leg; 3 tests (baseline disambiguation, stale-only-after-baseline fails loud, max-seq). commit 325d762. A residual-selection signature (newest-match on a persistent store + constant key, no pre-action floor), sibling of AF-AP-105; not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN .py code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-107 | A DETERMINISM GOLDEN OVER AN EVENT STREAM THAT CARRIES ASYNCHRONOUS NOTIFICATIONS — TWO IDENTICAL SAMPLES ARE A COIN TOSS, NOT PROOF — a "golden ×2 byte-identical after structure-preserving normalization" claim over a wire transcript that includes notifications the protocol does NOT order against the request/response stream (an agent's session-metadata update emitted from an independent task) passes whenever two samples happen to agree and fails on the third; the normalizer preserved ORDER for an event whose order is undefined, so the claim measured scheduling luck. Rule: a golden is defined over the sub-stream the protocol actually orders (a request, its response, the turn-bound chunks between them); an asynchronous notification is COUNTED (kind + count, key set) and never POSITIONED, and the claim states which events are order-free; N ≥ 3 samples before any "deterministic" word, the first disagreement a finding to classify (protocol-ordered = a real defect; unordered = the golden's definition), never a re-roll until two agree. | a golden/normalizer that keeps every notification in arrival order; `n1 == n2` over a full transcript with `session_info_update`/`usage_update`-class events; "identical ×2" with no third sample; a re-run "to get a matching pair" | 2026-09-21 (S0-01 VB-F12 recapture): the pinned hermes-acp's `session_info_update` landed BEFORE the reply chunk in run-1, AFTER the `end_turn` result in run-2, and BETWEEN chunk and usage in both 2026-09-05 golden runs — three samples, three positions; the frozen checker's `check_golden` (C:1533-1552, C:1608) reports `golden mismatch … at normalized line 7` and `last normalized line is not end_turn terminal`; the 2026-09-05 "golden x2 identical (11 lines)" was two matching rolls. | OPEN — an owner decision on the golden's definition (the normalizer treats asynchronous session-metadata notifications as order-free under a checker round with a red-first two-order test; alternatives: N-sample multiset equality, or a documented limit); the bundle is NOT minted on it. A capture-contract/normalizer signature (order-preserving normalization over unordered events), not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN `.py` code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-108 | A REMOTE SHELL COMMAND WHOSE LAST STATEMENT IS `[ -f X ] && …` RETURNS 1 WHEN X IS ABSENT, AND A `set -e`/`pipefail` CALLER READS THAT AS THE COMMAND'S FAILURE — a `for` loop of `[ -f sidecar ] && echo …` as the tail of a `bash scripts/pc.sh "…"` string makes the whole remote call's exit status the LAST test's status; when the last sidecar is absent (a leg that legitimately has none) the bridge returns 1 and the caller's `set -euo pipefail` aborts the step that had actually succeeded (the tar was written). Rule: an optional-presence branch in a remote/one-line command is an `if [ -f X ]; then …; fi` (status 0 either way) or ends with an explicit final status; a caller under set -e must know which statement's status it is consuming; a leg that carries a different file set than its siblings is the test case for every "optional" branch. | `… && for … do [ -f … ] && echo …; done"` as the tail of a remote command string; `[ -e X ] && cmd` as the LAST line of a script/heredoc consumed under `set -e`; a collect/pack step whose failure leaves the destination `rm -rf`'d and un-made | 2026-09-21 (S0-01 VB-F12 recapture, the first v2.4 negative take): `collect_leg.sh negative` packed the framedir (tgz 1482 bytes), the sidecar loop's last test failed (no manifests on the negative leg), pc.sh returned 1, the collect died before the fetch, `evidence/golden/negative` was deleted and never re-made; the 2026-09-06 negative had been collected by `realleg_sync.sh pc-build` instead, so the path had never run on a sidecar-less leg. | FIXED 2026-09-21 — the loop is `if … fi`; executable red-first control `test_collect_leg_sidecar_loop_exits_zero_when_a_leg_has_no_manifest_sidecars` (runs the un-escaped loop in a bare dir: status 0/no output, and with a sidecar: the `MANIFEST pre <sha>` line; RED `(1, '', '')` on 77f46a2's script). A shell-idiom signature; not an edit-snapshot AP_SCREEN `.py` code-hunk row. |
| AF-AP-109 | A REORDER CONTROL BUILT FROM SLICE ARITHMETIC THAT DUPLICATES THE NEIGHBOUR INSTEAD OF SWAPPING (`x[:i] + x[i+1:i+2] + x[i:i+1] + x[i+1:]` — the last slice must start at `i+2`): the "moved" list has one extra frame, so "the output changed" holds for the wrong reason and the control kills NOTHING it was written for (VERIFY-VB-F12 2026-09-21: the closed-set and the sync-order controls of the S0-01 golden let M4 "widen ASYNC_SESSION_UPDATES" and M8 "drop the async sort" survive the whole 476-test suite while the real checker's behaviour changed). Rule: a control MUTATION is itself verified before its effect is asserted — same length, same multiset (`sorted(moved) == sorted(orig)`), different order — and every "the output differs" control is paired with a named production mutant it must kill. No mechanical screen signature (slice arithmetic is legitimate elsewhere); the reviewer's check is the length/multiset assertion inside the control. | 2026-09-21 VERIFY-VB-F12 F1 |
| AF-AP-110 | AN ISOLATED-HOMEDIR gpg CALL LEAVES A gpg-agent DAEMON BEHIND — a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` used as `GNUPGHOME` for `gpg --import` / `--list-keys` / `--verify` auto-starts `gpg-agent --homedir <tmp> --use-standard-socket --daemon`; the directory is deleted on exit and the agent outlives it, ONE orphan per call (measured 2026-09-22 06:17Z on the real `verify_review`, `src/agent_factory/governance/review.py:128-149`: +1 agent per call; 26 orphans in the sandbox from the day’s runs, killed by pid). The TEST fixture kills its own agents (`gpgconf --kill all`, `tests/test_governance_review.py:70`) — the census that would have caught the production leak sat around the fixture, never around the function; `scripts/check-proof-status.py:146-157` (the sibling isolated-keyring path, through `git verify-tag`) measured +0 on one run — the difference is unexplained and is VERIFY-GOV2c item 8’s measurement. Rule: an isolated gpg homedir is torn down with `gpgconf --homedir <home> --kill all` BEFORE its directory is removed (or every call carries `--no-autostart` when it needs no agent), and the gate is a before/after process census around ONE production call — never the fixture’s own kill. Screen: `GNUPGHOME` assignment lines (advisory: name the teardown or the census). | 2026-09-22 coordinator (VERIFY-GOV2c authoring). RE-MEASURED 2026-09-22 10:50Z through the REAL function on both venues: 0→0 on the PC's GnuPG 2.4.7 (VERIFY-GOV2c-B item 8, every production-shaped call) and 0→0 on the sandbox's 2.4.4 (the 16 review tests, any `--homedir`) — the +1 did not reproduce with the current call shape; NO cleanup is built on it (a repair without a reproduction is hollow); re-open only with a reproduction through `verify_review` (issue #18) |
| AF-AP-111 | A ROUTE CLAIM STATED FROM CONFIG, NOT FROM THE EGRESS LOG — "the local-Qwen lane" named a combo whose step 2 is a cloud chain, and the local step refused long-context turns (`[400]: System message must be at the beginning.`, 343× on 2026-09-22 alone, first seen 2026-09-17T10:06Z at the vLLM cutover), so 57 % of the day’s successful build turns and 30 % of the verify turns ran on codex `gpt-5.6-*` at 100k-220k tokens while every ledger line, report header and effort record said Qwen; the tell came only when a cloud model’s SAFETY FILTER killed a verify lane (VERIFY-GOV2c 07:09Z).
